<!-- defn-1: body_md -->
For a vector $x\in\mathbb{Z}_q^n$, there are two relevant notions of its size.  The Hamming weight \begin{equation}
        |x|_{\mathrm{H}} := \sum_{i=1}^n (1-\delta_{x_i,0})
    \end{equation}
    counts the number of nonzero entries of $x$.  The Lee weight is \begin{equation}
        |x|_{\mathrm{L}} := \sum_{i=1}^n \min_{m_i\in \mathbb{Z}} |x_i-m_iq| =  \sum_{i=1}^n \min (x_i,q-x_i).
    \end{equation}
    In the last equality above, we are assuming that each $x_i\in\lbrace 0,1,\ldots, q-1\rbrace$.  The Lee distance between $x,y\in\mathbb{Z}_q^n$ is $|x-y|_{\mathrm{L}}$, and the resulting metric on $\mathbb{Z}_q^n$ is the Lee metric.  The Hamming distance is defined analogously.
<!-- end defn-1 -->
<!-- defn-2: body_md -->
\label{def:stabilizer}
A linear $[\![n,k,d_\diamond]\!]_q$ quantum stabilizer code on $\mathbb{Z}_q$ is specified
by a stabilizer matrix
\(
    H\in\mathbb{Z}_q^{m\times 2n}.
\)
Its $a$-th row is written as
\(
    h_a=(x_a\mid z_a)\in\mathbb{Z}_q^{2n}
\)
and lists the exponents of the stabilizer generator $X^{x_a}Z^{z_a}$, up to a phase.
Let
\begin{equation}
    J=
    \begin{pmatrix}
        0 & I_n \\
        -I_n & 0
    \end{pmatrix}.
\end{equation}
The stabilizer commutation condition is
\begin{equation}
    HJH^{\mathsf T}=0
    \quad(\mathrm{mod}\ q). \label{eq:stabcommute}
\end{equation}
The stabilizer group is $\mathcal{S}=\mathrm{im}(H^{\mathsf T})$, the logical group is
\begin{equation}
    \mathcal{L}=\ker(HJ)/\mathrm{im}(H^{\mathsf T}),
\end{equation}
and
\begin{equation}
    k=\frac{1}{2}\log_q|\mathcal{L}|=\log_q K,
    \qquad
    K=\frac{q^n}{|\mathcal{S}|}, \label{eq:stabk}
\end{equation}
where $K$ is the dimension of the codespace.  The joint Lee distance is
\begin{equation}
    d_\diamond=
    \min_{\ell\in\ker(HJ),\ \ell\notin\mathrm{im}(H^{\mathsf T})}
    |\ell|_{\mathrm{L}}, \label{eq:ddiamond}
\end{equation}
and the separate Lee distance is
\begin{equation}
    d_\square=
    \min_{\ell=(x\mid z)\in\ker(HJ),\ \ell\notin\mathrm{im}(H^{\mathsf T})}
    \max\left(|x|_{\mathrm{L}},|z|_{\mathrm{L}}\right). \label{eq:dsquare}
\end{equation}
<!-- end defn-2 -->
<!-- eg-1: body_md -->
\label{eg:z10}
Let $q=10$ and $n=1$, and let $H=(2\mid 4)$, so that the stabilizer group is generated by $X^2Z^4$.  This generator has order five, so $|\mathcal{S}|=5$, $K=2$, and $k=\log_{10}2$.  The syndrome of the error $X^aZ^b$ is $2b-4a$ modulo $10$, which is always even.  The five errors $I$, $X$, $X^{-1}$, $Z$, and $Z^{-1}$ have syndromes $0$, $6$, $4$, $2$, and $8$, respectively.  These are distinct and exhaust the five available syndrome values, so the code corrects every error of joint Lee weight one and is perfect in the sense of Section \ref{subsec:perfect}.  Errors of weight two are not corrected: $X^2$ shares its syndrome with $Z$, and $XZ$ shares its syndrome with $Z^{-1}$.  Accordingly, $d_\diamond=3$, and the weight-three operator $XZ^2$ is a logical operator.  Its conjugate logical operator $Z^5$ has Lee weight five.  In the separate metric, $XZ^2$ has weight two, so $d_\square=2$.
<!-- end eg-1 -->
<!-- eg-2: body_md -->
\label{eg:perfect643}
The $[\![6,4,3_\diamond]\!]_5$ code is generated by the two stabilizers
\begin{equation}
    \begin{aligned}
        S_1 &= Z\otimes Z\otimes XZ\otimes XZ^{2}\otimes X^{2}Z^{2}\otimes X^{-2}Z^{-2},\\
        S_2 &= X\otimes X^{2}Z\otimes X^{2}Z^{-2}\otimes X^{-1}\otimes XZ^{2}\otimes XZ^{2},
    \end{aligned}
    \label{eq:perfect643}
\end{equation}
i.e., by the rows $h_1=(0,0,1,1,2,-2\mid 1,1,1,2,2,-2)$ and $h_2=(1,2,2,-1,1,1\mid 0,1,-2,0,2,2)$ of $H$.  The symplectic product $h_1Jh_2^{\mathsf T}=-5$ vanishes modulo five, so the generators commute.  Each has order five, so $|\mathcal{S}|=25$, $K=5^6/25=5^4$, and $k=4$.  The Lee ball of radius one in $\mathbb{Z}_5^{12}$ contains $V_{12}(1)=25$ errors, one for each syndrome value, and their syndromes are distinct.  Thus, the code is perfect with $d_\diamond=3$.  It corrects any single $X^{\pm1}$ or $Z^{\pm1}$ error on any of its six qudits while encoding four qudits.
<!-- end eg-2 -->
<!-- eg-3: body_md -->
\label{eg:perfect3m}
Let $q=3m$ with $m\ge 2$.  The two stabilizers (with phases suppressed)
\begin{equation}
    S_1 = X^{m}\otimes (XZ)^{-m}, \qquad S_2 = (XZ)^{m}\otimes Z^{m}, \label{eq:perfect3m}
\end{equation}
with rows $h_1=(m,-m\mid 0,-m)$ and $h_2=(m,0\mid m,m)$, commute over the integers, and each has order three.  Thus, $\mathcal{S}\cong\mathbb{Z}_3^2$ for every $m$, and $K=(3m)^2/9=m^2$.  The syndrome of the error $(a\mid b)$ is
\begin{equation}
    HJ(a\mid b)^{\mathsf T} = m\,\big(b_1-b_2+a_2,\ b_1-a_1-a_2\big) \quad (\mathrm{mod}\ 3m),
\end{equation}
which depends only on the error modulo three.  The nine errors of joint Lee weight at most one map onto the nine possible values in $m\mathbb{Z}_{3m}^2\cong\mathbb{Z}_3^2$, so the code is perfect with $d_\diamond=3$.  The undetectable error $X_1^3$ is not a stabilizer for any $m\ge2$, and it is a logical operator of weight three.  For $m=2$, the code is a $[\![2,\,2\log_6 2,\,3_\diamond]\!]_6$ code that stores a four-dimensional logical space in two six-dimensional qudits.
<!-- end eg-3 -->
<!-- defn-3: body_md -->
\label{def : Classical_linear_Code}
    A parity-check matrix $H\in \mathbb{Z}_q^{m\times n}$ defines an $[n,k,d]_q$ code, with codespace $\mathcal{C}=\ker(H)$.   Define $ k := \log_q |\mathcal{C}|$  as the ``number" of logical variables.  $k$ need not be an integer.  The code distance $d$ is the minimum Lee weight of a nonzero codeword: \begin{equation}
        d := \min_{x\in \mathcal{C}\setminus0} |x|_{\mathrm{L}}. \label{eq:classical_d}
    \end{equation}
<!-- end defn-3 -->
<!-- prop-1: body_md -->
\label{prop:classicalcodespace}
    Let $\{e_i\}_{i=1}^n$ be the canonical basis of $\mathbb{Z}_q^n$.  Given the Smith factors $d_i$ of the parity-check matrix $H$, the codespace is \begin{equation}
        \mathcal{C} = \mathrm{span}\left\lbrace \widehat{d}_i \cdot Ve_i \right\rbrace_{i=1}^n \cong \mathbb{Z}_q^{n-r}\oplus \bigoplus_{j=1}^r \mathbb{Z}_{\gcd(q,d_j)}  . \label{eq:classicalcodespace}
    \end{equation}
<!-- end prop-1 -->
<!-- prop-2: body_md -->
For $q$ prime, the codespace is $\mathcal{C}\cong\mathbb{Z}_q^{n-r+s}$, where $s$ is the number of Smith factors $d_i$ that are multiples of $q$.  In particular, $\mathcal{C}$ is a vector space with integer dimension, so the number of logical dits $k$ is an integer.\footnote{This is a consequence of the fact that $\mathbb{Z}_q$ is a finite field, not just a ring.  In the coding literature it is thus usually denoted as $\mathbb{F}_q$.}
<!-- end prop-2 -->
<!-- defn-4: body_md -->
A linear $[\![n,k,d]\!]_q$ quantum CSS code on $\mathbb{Z}_q$ is given by matrices $H_X\in \mathbb{Z}_q^{m_X\times n}$ and $H_Z\in\mathbb{Z}_q^{m_Z\times n}$ obeying \begin{equation}
        H_XH_Z^{\mathsf{T}} = 0\ \text{(mod $q$)}.
    \end{equation}
    $k$ is defined analogously to Definition~\ref{def : Classical_linear_Code}: \begin{equation}
        k = \log_q \frac{|\ker(H_X)|}{|\mathrm{im}(H_Z^{\mathsf{T}})|}= \log_q \frac{|\ker(H_Z)|}{|\mathrm{im}(H_X^{\mathsf{T}})|}. \label{eq:quantumk}
    \end{equation}
    The Lee distances $d_X$ and $d_Z$ are defined analogously to \eqref{eq:classical_d}: \begin{subequations}\begin{align}
        d_X &= \min_{x\in \ker(H_Z), \;  x\notin \mathrm{im}(H_X^{\mathsf{T}})}|x|_{\mathrm{L}},\\
        d_Z &= \min_{z\in \ker(H_X), \;  z\notin \mathrm{im}(H_Z^{\mathsf{T}})}|z|_{\mathrm{L}}.
    \end{align}\end{subequations}
    The Lee distance of the code is $d=\min(d_X,d_Z)$.
<!-- end defn-4 -->
<!-- thm-1: body_md -->
\label{thm:quantumcodespace}
Consider the quantum code defined by $H_X : \mathbb{Z}_q^n \rightarrow \mathbb{Z}_q^{m_X}$ and $H_Z: \mathbb{Z}_q^n \rightarrow \mathbb{Z}_q^{m_Z}$ obeying $H_XH_Z^{\mathsf{T}}=0$ on $\mathbb{Z}_q$.  Regard $H_X$ and $H_Z$ as integer matrices by choosing integer lifts of their entries. By the isomorphisms below, the result does not depend on this choice.  Define the abstract chain complex over $\mathbb{Z}$ given by three matrices $D_{1,2,3}$ obeying \begin{equation}
    D_1D_2 = D_2D_3=0,
\end{equation}
where the integer-valued matrices $D_{1,2,3}$ are:\footnote{Because $H_XH_Z^{\mathsf{T}}=0\; \text{(mod $q$)}$, the matrix $q^{-1}H_XH_Z^{\mathsf{T}}$ has integer entries.} \begin{subequations}
    \begin{align}
        D_1 &= \left(\begin{array}{cc} H_X &\ - qI_{m_X} \end{array}\right), \label{eq:D1def} \\
        D_2 &= \left(\begin{array}{cc} H_Z^{\mathsf{T}} &\ qI_{n} \\ q^{-1}H_XH_Z^{\mathsf{T}} &\ H_X \end{array}\right), \label{eq:D2def} \\
        D_3 &= \left(\begin{array}{c} qI_{m_Z} \\ -H_Z^{\mathsf{T}} \end{array}\right).
    \end{align}
\end{subequations}
The codespace of the quantum code is \begin{align}
    \mathcal{C} &:= \ker_{\mathbb{Z}_q} (H_X)/\mathrm{im}_{\mathbb{Z}_q}(H_Z^{\mathsf{T}}) \cong \ker_{\mathbb{Z}}(D_1)/\mathrm{im}_{\mathbb{Z}}(D_2)  \notag \\
    &\cong \ker_{\mathbb{Z}_q} (H_Z)/\mathrm{im}_{\mathbb{Z}_q}(H_X^{\mathsf{T}}) \cong \ker_{\mathbb{Z}}(D_3^{\mathsf{T}})/\mathrm{im}_{\mathbb{Z}}(D_2^{\mathsf{T}}).
\end{align}
Following Proposition~\ref{prop:classicalcodespace}, we call $\mathcal{C}$ the codespace. Its order $|\mathcal{C}|=q^k$ is the dimension of the code's Hilbert space. It should not be confused with the logical group $\mathcal{C}$ of Definition~\ref{def:stabilizer}, whose order is $q^{2k}$.
Moreover, the Smith decomposition of $D_2$ takes the form \begin{equation} \label{eq:D2SNF}
    D_2 = \widetilde{U}\left(\begin{array}{cc} \widetilde{D}_{n\times n} &\ 0_{n\times m_Z} \\ 0_{m_X\times n} &\ 0 _{m_X\times m_Z} \end{array}\right)\widetilde{V},
\end{equation}
where the subscripts give the dimensions of each block of the middle matrix, and $\widetilde{D} = \mathrm{diag}(d_1,\ldots, d_n)$ has nonzero factors $1 \le d_1,\ldots, d_n\le q$, with each $d_j \; | \; q$. The codespace is \begin{equation}
    \mathcal{C} \cong \bigoplus_{i=1}^n \mathbb{Z}_{d_i}. \label{eq:quantumcodespace}
\end{equation}
Notice that any factor with $d_i=1$ does not contribute meaningfully to the codespace. For any $d_j>1$ in the SNF $\widetilde{D}$,  a generator for the corresponding $Z$-logical operator is given by \begin{equation}
    \left( z_{\mathrm{L}}^{(j)}\right)_a = \widetilde{U}_{a,j} \; \text{(mod $q$)} \;\;\; (a=1,\ldots,n), \label{eq:zLogical}
\end{equation}
where $\widetilde{U}_{a,j}$ are the appropriate matrix elements of $\widetilde{U}\in \mathbb{Z}^{(n+m_X) \times (n+m_X)}$. The conjugate $X$-logical operator is \begin{equation}
    \left( x_{\mathrm{L}}^{(j)}\right)_a = \widetilde{V}_{j,m_Z+a} \; \text{(mod $q$)} \;\;\; (a=1,\ldots,n). \label{eq:xLogical}
\end{equation}
As vectors in $\mathbb{Z}_q^n$, these logical operators generate a logical $\mathbb{Z}_{d_j}$-qudit: on $\mathbb{Z}_q$, \begin{equation}
    x_{\mathrm{L}}^{(j)} \cdot z_{\mathrm{L}}^{(k)} = \frac{q}{d_j} \delta_{jk}. \label{eq:logicalConjugates}
\end{equation}
<!-- end thm-1 -->
<!-- eg-4: body_md -->
\label{eg:toruscode}
    Consider the case $m_X=m_Z=n=1$ for positive integers $a,b,c$ with $c\mid ab$ and $q=ab/c\ge2$.    Now consider $H_X = a$ and $H_Z =  b$.   $H_XH_Z^{\mathsf{T}} = ab = 0 \; (\text{mod $q$})$, so this is a valid CSS code.  We can explicitly carry out the procedure in Theorem \ref{thm:quantumcodespace}: the matrix \begin{equation}
        D_2 = \left(\begin{array}{cc} b &\ q \\ c &\ a \end{array}\right) = \left(\begin{array}{cc} q/a^\prime &\ a_1 \\ a/a^\prime &\ a_2 \end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}{cc} c^\prime &\ 0 \\ 0 &\ 0 \end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}{cc} ba^\prime/qc^\prime &\ a^\prime/c^\prime \\ b_1 &\ b_2 \end{array}\right),
    \end{equation}
    where $c^\prime = \gcd(a,b,c,q)$, $a^\prime = \gcd(a,q)$, $b^\prime = \gcd(b,q)$, and $a_{1,2}$ and $b_{1,2}$ are B\'ezout coefficients chosen to ensure that $\widetilde{U}$ and $\widetilde{V}$ are unimodular.  We deduce that the codespace is $\mathcal{C}\cong\mathbb{Z}_{c^\prime}$ and that the logical operators correspond to $z_{\mathrm{L}} = q/a^\prime$ and $x_{\mathrm{L}} = a^\prime/c^\prime$.  A short exercise 
shows that $a^\prime/c^\prime = q/b^\prime$.

The logical operators can also be recovered directly from the stabilizer
conditions. A logical $X$ operator lies in $\ker(H_Z)$, which is generated by
$q/b'$, and hence one may take $x_{\mathrm{L}}=q/b'$. Since $q/b'=a'/c'$, this agrees with
the logical operator obtained from Theorem \ref{thm:quantumcodespace}.

    
This example is the finite-dimensional analogue of the ideal
single-mode GKP code, whose lattice is square when $a'=b'$ and which encodes a qubit when $c'=2$. For a bosonic mode with canonical quadratures
$[\hat q,\hat p]=\mathrm{i}$, the displacement operator
\begin{equation} \label{eq:displacementconvention}
    D(\Delta q,\Delta p)
    =
    \exp\!\left[\mathrm{i}(\Delta p\,\hat q-\Delta q\,\hat p)\right]
\end{equation}
translates a state by $(\Delta q,\Delta p)$ in phase space. The square GKP code
is the simultaneous $+1$ eigenspace of the commuting displacements
$D(2\sqrt{\pi},0)$ and $D(0,2\sqrt{\pi})$, while the half-lattice displacements
$D(\sqrt{\pi},0)$ and $D(0,\sqrt{\pi})$ act as logical $X$ and logical $Z$,
respectively. Here the continuous phase plane is replaced by the discrete torus
$\mathbb Z_q\times \mathbb Z_q$. The stabilizers $X^a$ and $Z^b$ generate the
two discrete lattice translations, while the logical operators $Z^{q/a'}$ and
$X^{a'/c'}=X^{q/b'}$ generate the residual finite quotient. Small Pauli shifts
correspond to short translations on the discrete torus, whose cyclic displacement
is measured by the Lee metric. The corresponding exact single-mode GKP realization is given in Section \ref{subsec:gkp} after the general CSS/GKP correspondence is established.

    In the notation of Section \ref{sec:stabilizer}, this is a $[\![1,\log_q c',d]\!]_q$ code.  The $X$-type logical operators are the powers of $X^{q/b'}$ that are not powers of $X^{a'}$, and likewise for $Z$, so $d_X=q/b'$ and $d_Z=q/a'$ whenever $c'>1$, and $d=\min(q/a',q/b')$.  The syndromes of $X$-type errors take $q/b'$ values, and those of $Z$-type errors take $q/a'$ values.  The code is therefore perfect in the sense of Section \ref{subsec:perfect} exactly when $a'=b'$ and $q/a'=2t+1$ is odd.  In that case, the $2t+1$ errors of each type with Lee weight at most $t$ exhaust the syndrome values of that type.  In particular, the choice $a=b=K(2t+1)$ and $q=K(2t+1)^2$ gives $a'=b'=K(2t+1)$ and $c'=K$, and yields the perfect $[\![1,\log_qK,(2t+1)]\!]_q$ codes of Table \ref{tab:perfect}. The codes of this example are the shift-resistant codes of Ref.~\cite{Gottesman2001GKP}, which already noted that some of them are perfect. Members of this family that encode a qubit were studied in Refs.~\cite{Pirandola2008minimal,Cafaro2012embedding}.
<!-- end eg-4 -->
<!-- eg-5: body_md -->
\label{eg:perfectcss2}
    Let $q=m(2t^2+2t+1)$ with $m\ge2$ and $t\ge1$, and let $H_X=H_Z=(mt,\ m(t+1))$, so that the checks are $X^{mt}\otimes X^{m(t+1)}$ and $Z^{mt}\otimes Z^{m(t+1)}$.  The CSS condition holds because $m^2t^2+m^2(t+1)^2=mq$.  Each check has order $2t^2+2t+1$, so the logical dimension is $K=q^2/(2t^2+2t+1)^2=m^2$ and $k=2\log_q m$.  The syndrome of an $X$-error $a\in\mathbb{Z}_q^2$ is $m\varphi(a)$, where $\varphi(a)=ta_1+(t+1)a_2$ is taken modulo $2t^2+2t+1$.  The kernel of $\varphi$ on $\mathbb{Z}^2$ is the lattice generated by the orthogonal vectors $(t,t+1)$ and $(-(t+1),t)$, whose nonzero elements have $1$-norm at least $2t+1$.  Thus, $\varphi$ is injective on the Lee ball of radius $t$, which has exactly as many elements as there are syndrome values.  This is the two-dimensional Lee-ball tiling of Ref.~\cite{GolombWelch1970}, and the code is a perfect $[\![2,2\log_qm,(2t+1)]\!]_q$ code.  The vector $(t+1,-t)$ lies in the kernel of $\varphi$ and is not a stabilizer for $m\ge2$, so $d_X=d_Z=2t+1$ exactly.

    The member with $t=1$ and $m=2$ is a $[\![2,2\log_{10}2,3]\!]_{10}$ code with checks $X^2\otimes X^4$ and $Z^2\otimes Z^4$.  Its Hamming distance is one, since $X_1^5$ commutes with both checks and is not a stabilizer.  This operator squares to the identity and is therefore Hermitian, so $\exp(\mathrm{i}\theta X_1^5)=\cos\theta+\mathrm{i}\sin\theta\,X_1^5$ is a transversal unitary that preserves the codespace and acts on it as a nontrivial logical gate for generic $\theta$. The code thus admits a continuous family of transversal logical gates, with the Lee weight of the generator \(X_1^5\) being at least the Lee distance by definition.  The Eastin--Knill theorem~\cite{EastinKnill2009,Faist2020continuous} forbids such a family for any code that detects every single-qudit error in the Hamming metric. Its conclusion does not extend to codes required to detect only small Lee-weight errors: a code can correct every error of Lee weight one, as this one does, while having Hamming distance one.
<!-- end eg-5 -->
<!-- eg-6: body_md -->
\label{eg:perfectcssn}
    Let $q=2n+1$ with $n\ge3$ and $n\not\equiv1\pmod 3$, and let $H_X=H_Z=(1,2,\dots,n)$, so that the checks are $X\otimes X^2\otimes\cdots\otimes X^n$ and $Z\otimes Z^2\otimes\cdots\otimes Z^n$.  The CSS condition requires $\sum_{j=1}^nj^2=n(n+1)(2n+1)/6$ to vanish modulo $2n+1$, which holds if and only if $6$ divides $n(n+1)$, i.e., exactly for these $n$.  Each check has order $q$, so $K=q^{n-2}$ and $k=n-2$.  The syndromes of the $X$-errors of Lee weight at most one, of which there are $2n+1$, are $0$ and $\pm j$ for $j=1,\dots,n$, which are distinct modulo $2n+1$ and exhaust $\mathbb{Z}_q$.  The code is thus a perfect $[\![n,n-2,3]\!]_{2n+1}$ code, and its classical ingredient is the radius-one perfect Lee code of Ref.~\cite{GolombWelch1970}.  Moreover, $X_1^2X_2^{-1}$ is a logical operator of Lee weight three that is not a stabilizer for $n\ge3$, so $d_X=d_Z=3$ exactly.  The first members are the $[\![3,1,3]\!]_7$, $[\![5,3,3]\!]_{11}$, and $[\![6,4,3]\!]_{13}$ codes.
<!-- end eg-6 -->
<!-- eg-7: body_md -->
\label{eg:liftedhamming}
    Let $q=2^r$, and let $\lambda\in\mathbb{Z}_q$ satisfy $\lambda^2-\lambda+2=0$, e.g., $\lambda=0,2,6,6,6,38$ for $q=2,4,8,16,32,64$.  The cyclic code $\mathcal{H}_q$ of length seven generated by (using polynomial notation for cyclic codes~\cite{macwilliams1977theory})
    \begin{equation}
        g_q(x)=x^3+\lambda x^2+(\lambda-1)x-1 \label{eq:liftedhamming}
    \end{equation}
    is the Hensel lift of the $[7,4,3]$ Hamming code, to which it reduces modulo two.  It is also the puncturing of the extended code of Ref.~\cite{CalderbankSloane1995} at one coordinate.  Its dual is generated by $(x-1)g_q(x)$ and is contained in $\mathcal{H}_q$.  The CSS construction with $H_X=H_Z$ given by the parity-check matrix of $\mathcal{H}_q$ therefore yields a $[\![7,1,d]\!]_q$ code, where $d=d_X=d_Z$ is the minimum Lee weight of $\mathcal{H}_q\setminus\mathcal{H}_q^\perp$.  By exhaustive enumeration, we find
    \begin{equation}
        d=3,\,4,\,5,\,7,\,7,\,7 \qquad \text{for} \qquad q=2,\,4,\,8,\,16,\,32,\,64. \label{eq:liftedhammingd}
    \end{equation}
    The $q=4$ member is the quantum heptacode $[\![7,1,4]\!]_4$, which stems from the octacode.
The distance cannot exceed seven for any $q$.  Indeed, the all-ones vector lies in $\mathcal{H}_q$, because $g_q$ divides $1+x+\cdots+x^6$, but not in $\mathcal{H}_q^\perp$, because $g_q(1)=2\lambda-1$ is a unit.  Thus, the growth of the quantum distance with $q$ saturates at $q=16$, in contrast with the classical Lee distance of the extended codes, which keeps growing.  Shortening the extended code at two coordinates instead of one yields a $[\![6,2,3]\!]_4$ code for $q=4$.
<!-- end eg-7 -->
<!-- eg-8: body_md -->
\label{eg:liftedgolay}
    The cyclic code of length $23$ over $\mathbb{Z}_4$ generated by
    \begin{equation}
        \pi_4(x)=x^{11}+2x^{10}+3x^{9}+3x^{7}+3x^{6}+3x^{5}+2x^{4}+x+3 \label{eq:liftedgolay}
    \end{equation}
    is the Hensel lift of the $[23,12,7]$ Golay code.  Its extension by one coordinate is the self-dual $\mathbb{Z}_4$ Golay code~\cite{Bonnecaze1995}, whose Lee distance is $12$.  Like its binary counterpart, the cyclic code contains its dual, which is generated by $(x-1)\pi_4(x)$, and the CSS construction yields a $[\![23,1,10]\!]_4$ code.  Shortening the extended code at two coordinates yields a $[\![22,2,8]\!]_4$ code.  For higher powers of two, the lifts of Ref.~\cite{CalderbankSloane1995} yield $[\![23,1,12]\!]_8$ and $[\![23,1,17]\!]_{16}$ codes, whose distances we computed by exhaustive enumeration.  As for the Hamming family, the all-ones vector is a logical operator, so the distance of this family never exceeds $23$.
<!-- end eg-8 -->
<!-- eg-9: body_md -->
\label{eg:rs5}
    Let $q=5$, and let $H_X=H_Z$ have the rows $(1,1,1,1,1)$ and $(0,1,2,3,4)$.  These rows are the evaluations of the polynomials $1$ and $x$ at the five points of $\mathbb{Z}_5$.  They span the Reed--Solomon code $\mathrm{RS}_2$ of polynomials of degree less than two, whose dual is the code $\mathrm{RS}_3$ of polynomials of degree less than three.  The CSS construction yields a quantum Reed--Solomon code~\cite{AharonovBenOr2008,Cleve1999secret,Grassl2004optimal}, which encodes one qudit.

    Its nontrivial logical operators are the evaluations of polynomials of degree two, modulo $\mathrm{RS}_2$.  Its Hamming distance is three, but by exhaustive enumeration its Lee distance is four.  It is thus a $[\![5,1,4]\!]_5$ code. By the sphere-packing bound \eqref{eq:spherepackingsquare}, no nondegenerate CSS code with these parameters has a larger Lee distance.  Example \ref{eg:rs5ft} discusses its transversal gates.
<!-- end eg-9 -->
<!-- prop-3: body_md -->
The logical-operator quotient of the multimode GKP code described by \eqref{eq:GKPlattice} is isomorphic to that of the corresponding $\mathbb Z_q$ code,
\begin{equation} \label{eq:GKP_codespace}
    \frac{\Lambda_q(\mathcal S_{\mathrm{CSS}})^{\perp_{2\pi}}}
    {\Lambda_q(\mathcal S_{\mathrm{CSS}})}
    \cong
    \frac{\ker(H_Z)}{\operatorname{im}(H_X^{\mathsf T})}
    \oplus
    \frac{\ker(H_X)}{\operatorname{im}(H_Z^{\mathsf T})}.
\end{equation}
More explicitly, there is a one-to-one correspondence between the logical classes of the two codes: every logical operator $v_q\in \mathcal S_{\mathrm{CSS}}^\perp/\mathcal S_{\mathrm{CSS}}$ of the $\mathbb Z_q$ code can be mapped to a unique logical operator of the multimode GKP code\begin{equation} \label{eq:vGKP}
    v_{\text{GKP}} = \lambda_q \pi_q^{-1}(v_q) \in \frac{\Lambda_q(\mathcal S_{\mathrm{CSS}})^{\perp_{2\pi}}}
    {\Lambda_q(\mathcal S_{\mathrm{CSS}})},
\end{equation}
and their reduced weights, i.e., the minimum weights over all representatives of the logical class, are proportional to each other \begin{equation} \label{eq:GKPreducedweight}
    \min_{u\in \Lambda_q(\mathcal S_{\mathrm{CSS}})}
    \left|v_{\text{GKP}}+u\right|_1
    =
    \lambda_q
    \min_{w\in \mathcal S_{\mathrm{CSS}}}
    |v_q+w|_{\mathrm L}.
\end{equation}
<!-- end prop-3 -->
<!-- proof-1: body_md -->
Since $\lambda_q qe_k\in\Lambda_q(\mathcal S_{\mathrm{CSS}})$ for every standard basis vector $e_k$ of $\mathbb R^{2n}$, and $\lambda_q^2q=2\pi$, every $\eta\in\Lambda_q(\mathcal S_{\mathrm{CSS}})^{\perp_{2\pi}}$ lies in $\lambda_q\mathbb Z^{2n}$. Writing $\eta=\lambda_q v$ and $\xi=\lambda_q u$, the condition $\eta J\xi^{\mathsf T}\in2\pi\mathbb Z$ becomes $vJu^{\mathsf T}\equiv0\pmod q$, which gives \eqref{eq:lambdaperp} and, with \eqref{eq:S_SCC_logical}, \eqref{eq:GKP_codespace}. Since $\lambda_q q\mathbb Z^{2n}\subseteq\Lambda_q(\mathcal S_{\mathrm{CSS}})$, the minimum in \eqref{eq:GKPreducedweight} runs over all integer lifts of the classes $v_q+w$, and $\min_{x\equiv a\ (\mathrm{mod}\ q)}|x|=|a|_{\mathrm L}$ entrywise.
<!-- end proof-1 -->
<!-- defn-5: body_md -->
\label{def:leespread}
    The Lee error spread of a Clifford gate $U$ with symplectic matrix $M$ is
    \begin{equation}
        w_{\mathrm B}(U) := \max_{p\in\mathbb{Z}_q^{2n}\setminus 0}\frac{|Mp|_{\mathrm L}}{|p|_{\mathrm L}}, \label{eq:leespread}
    \end{equation}
    the largest factor by which conjugation by $U$ can increase the joint Lee weight \eqref{eq:jointlee} of a Pauli string.
<!-- end defn-5 -->
<!-- lem-1: body_md -->
\label{lem:spread}
    For every matrix $M\in\mathbb{Z}_q^{m\times N}$,
    \begin{equation}
        \max_{p\in\mathbb{Z}_q^{N}\setminus0}\frac{|Mp|_{\mathrm L}}{|p|_{\mathrm L}}=\max_{1\le j\le N}|Me_j|_{\mathrm L}, \label{eq:columnformula}
    \end{equation}
    where $e_j$ is the $j$-th standard basis vector.  The same holds for integer matrices with the $1$-norm in place of the Lee weight.  In particular, $w_{\mathrm B}(U)$ is a positive integer, namely the largest Lee weight of a column of the symplectic matrix of $U$.
<!-- end lem-1 -->
<!-- proof-2: body_md -->
Lee weight is subadditive and invariant under $v\mapsto-v$.  Every $p$ is a sum of $|p|_{\mathrm L}$ vectors of the form $\pm e_j$, because its $j$-th entry equals $\pm|p_j|_{\mathrm L}$ modulo $q$.  Hence $|Mp|_{\mathrm L}\le\sum_j|p_j|_{\mathrm L}\,|Me_j|_{\mathrm L}\le|p|_{\mathrm L}\max_j|Me_j|_{\mathrm L}$.  Equality holds for $p=e_j$ with $j$ a maximizing index.  The same argument applies to the $1$-norm on $\mathbb{Z}^N$.
<!-- end proof-2 -->
<!-- eg-10: body_md -->
\label{eg:rs5ft}
    Consider the $[\![5,1,4]\!]_5$ code of Example \ref{eg:rs5}.  Every single-qudit Clifford gate acts transversally on it, since $H_X=H_Z$.  The transversal gates thus realize the full Clifford group of the logical qudit.  Modulo Pauli operators and phases, this group is $\mathrm{Sp}(2,\mathbb{Z}_5)=\mathrm{SL}(2,5)$, which is isomorphic to the binary icosahedral group $2I$ of order $120$.  The smallest known qubit code whose transversal gates form the group $2I$ is the $((7,2,3))$ code of Pollatsek and Ruskai~\cite{Pollatsek2004permutation,Kubischta2023exotic}.  The two codes are otherwise not directly comparable, since our five subsystems have dimension five and our distance is measured in the Lee metric.  For quantum polynomial codes over prime fields, Aharonov and Ben-Or implemented the $\mathrm{SUM}$ and multiplication gates transversally, and the Fourier gate up to a change of code~\cite{AharonovBenOr2008}.

    The distance $d=4$ gives $t=1$.  The gates $F^{\otimes5}$, $S^{\otimes5}$, and $\mathrm{SUM}^{\otimes5}$ between two blocks each tolerate one fault.  A single fault before $\mathrm{MUL}_2^{\otimes5}$ becomes $X_j^{\pm2}$ or $Z_j^{\pm2}$, of Lee weight two.  This error has the same syndrome as an error of Lee weight two on two other qudits, and the two differ by a logical operator.  Whether $\mathrm{MUL}_2^{\otimes5}$ tolerates one fault thus depends on how the decoder breaks this tie.  For any decoder that corrects every error of Lee weight one, the logical $\mathrm{MUL}_2$ gate tolerates one fault when it is implemented as a product of $F^{\otimes5}$ and $S^{\otimes5}$ gates, with error correction between them.
<!-- end eg-10 -->
<!-- eg-11: body_md -->
\label{eg:perfectcss2ft}
    Consider the codes of Example \ref{eg:perfectcss2}, with $q=m(2t^2+2t+1)$, $H_X=H_Z=(mt,\ m(t+1))$, and distance $d=2t+1$.  Let $a_1=(t,t+1)$ and $a_2=(-(t+1),t)$.  Since $a_i\cdot a_j=\delta_{ij}\,q/m$, the operators $X^{a_i}$ and $Z^{a_i}$ act as the Pauli operators of two logical qudits of dimension $m$.

    Because $H_X=H_Z$, the Fourier gate $F^{\otimes2}$ on both qudits exchanges the $X$ checks and the $Z$ checks, up to inversion.  It is a logical gate of spread one, and it acts as the Fourier gate on each logical qudit.  The phase gate $S^{\otimes2}$ on both qudits fixes each $Z$ check and maps each $X$ check to the product of an $X$ check and a $Z$ check.  The resulting logical gate has spread two and acts as a logical phase gate on each logical qudit, up to a logical Pauli string.

    At fixed $m$, the radius $t$ grows with $q$, since $(2t+1)^2=2q/m-1$, so that $t\approx\sqrt{q/(2m)}$.  By \eqref{eq:faultdistance}, every logical Clifford gate $U$ of spread $w=w_{\mathrm B}(U)$ satisfies
    \begin{equation}
        d_{\mathrm{fault}}(U)\ge\left\lceil\frac{\sqrt{2q/m-1}}{2w}\right\rceil. \label{eq:perfectcss2ft}
    \end{equation}
    Such a gate tolerates at least one fault once $t\ge w$, that is, once $q\ge m(2w^2+2w+1)$.  At fixed $m$ and $w$, the bound grows as $\sqrt q$, while each fault keeps Lee weight one.

    Like every transversal phase gate on a CSS code, $S^{\otimes2}$ tolerates $t$ faults, as many as a gate of spread one.  This is optimal.  After any gate, $t+1$ faults can produce the error $X_2^{t+1}$.  It has the same syndrome as $X_1^{-t}$, so the decoder turns it into the logical operator $X^{a_1}$.  Hence $d_{\mathrm{fault}}(F^{\otimes2})=d_{\mathrm{fault}}(S^{\otimes2})=t+1$.

    The GKP realization of Section \ref{subsec:gkp} turns these codes and gates into standard GKP objects.  The vectors $a_1$ and $a_2$ are orthogonal and have length $\sqrt{q/m}$, so $a_i=\sqrt{q/m}\,R_\theta e_i$.  Here $R_\theta$ is the rotation of the plane by the angle $\theta=\arctan((t+1)/t)$.  The integer lifts of the stabilizer labels of each type form the lattice generated by $ma_1$ and $ma_2$.  The GKP stabilizer lattice \eqref{eq:GKPlattice} is therefore
    \begin{equation}
        \Lambda_q(\mathcal S_{\mathrm{CSS}})=\sqrt{2\pi m}\,\left(R_\theta\oplus R_\theta\right)\mathbb Z^4. \label{eq:perfectcss2gkp}
    \end{equation}
    The map $R_\theta\oplus R_\theta$ rotates the position displacements $(\Delta q_1,\Delta q_2)$ and the momentum displacements $(\Delta p_1,\Delta p_2)$ in the same way, so it is a beam splitter $B_\theta$.  The GKP realization is thus $B_\theta$ applied to two $m$-dimensional square GKP qudits, with stabilizers $D(\sqrt{2\pi m}\,e_j,0)$ and $D(0,\sqrt{2\pi m}\,e_j)$~\cite{Gottesman2001GKP}.  The logical Pauli operators $X^{a_i}$ and $Z^{a_i}$ become the images under $B_\theta$ of the Pauli operators $D(\sqrt{2\pi/m}\,e_i,0)$ and $D(0,\sqrt{2\pi/m}\,e_i)$ of the square GKP qudits.

    The gates follow the same pattern.  In the GKP realization, $F^{\otimes2}$ rotates the phase space of each mode by a quarter turn, $(\Delta q_j,\Delta p_j)\mapsto(-\Delta p_j,\Delta q_j)$.  Up to a displacement, $S^{\otimes2}$ is the shear $(\Delta q_j,\Delta p_j)\mapsto(\Delta q_j,\Delta q_j+\Delta p_j)$ of each mode.  These Gaussian gates do not depend on the dimension of a square GKP qudit.  They are the Fourier gate and the phase gate both for the $q$-dimensional qudits of the grid and for the $m$-dimensional square GKP qudits.  They act on both modes in the same way, so they commute with $B_\theta$.  The physical gates $F^{\otimes2}$ and $S^{\otimes2}$ are thus the same Gaussian unitaries as the logical Fourier and phase gates of the square GKP qudits, for every $t$.  Similarly, swapping the two qudits and then inverting the second one is the beam splitter $B_{-\pi/2}$, which commutes with $B_\theta$.  It is a logical gate of spread one, and it swaps the two square GKP qudits up to an inversion.

    As $q\to\infty$ at fixed $m$, the angle $\theta$ tends to $\pi/4$, since $\tan(\theta-\pi/4)=1/(2t+1)$.  The GKP realizations thus converge to two $m$-dimensional square GKP qudits mixed by a balanced (50:50) beam splitter.  This limit code has the stabilizer lattice $\sqrt{\pi m}\,(D_2\oplus D_2)$, where $D_2=\{(u,v)\in\mathbb Z^2:u+v\text{ even}\}$ is the checkerboard lattice.  Its stabilizer group is generated by $D(\sqrt{\pi m}\,(e_1\pm e_2),0)$ and $D(0,\sqrt{\pi m}\,(e_1\pm e_2))$.  In the limit, the Lee-sphere tiling of Example \ref{eg:perfectcss2} becomes the tiling of each quadrature plane by $1$-norm balls of radius $\sqrt{\pi/m}$ centered on the logical lattice $\sqrt{\pi/m}\,D_2$.  Each fault of Lee weight one is a displacement by one grid step $\lambda_q=\sqrt{2\pi/q}$, which tends to zero while the GKP realizations converge.
<!-- end eg-11 -->
<!-- lem-2: body_md -->
\label{lem:graydictionary}
    Let $R=F^2$ be the inversion $|m\rangle\mapsto|-m\rangle$ of one quartrit, so that $R=\tfrac12(I+X^2+Z^2-X^2Z^2)$.  Under $\phi_Z$, the qubit Pauli operators pull back as $E\mapsto\phi_Z^\dagger E\phi_Z$, namely
    \begin{subequations} \label{eq:graydictionary}
    \begin{align}
        \sigma_x\otimes I&\mapsto X^{-1}R, &
        I\otimes\sigma_x&\mapsto XR, &
        \sigma_x\otimes\sigma_x&\mapsto X^2, \label{eq:graydictionaryx}\\
        \sigma_z\otimes I&\mapsto \frac{e^{-i\pi/4}Z+e^{i\pi/4}Z^{-1}}{\sqrt2}, &
        I\otimes\sigma_z&\mapsto \frac{e^{i\pi/4}Z+e^{-i\pi/4}Z^{-1}}{\sqrt2}, &
        \sigma_z\otimes\sigma_z&\mapsto Z^2. \label{eq:graydictionaryz}
    \end{align}
    \end{subequations}
    Consequently, a qubit Pauli string on $2n$ qubits pulls back to
    \begin{equation}
        \Phi_Z^\dagger\,\sigma_x^{a}\sigma_z^{b}\,\Phi_Z = X^{t}R_{\mathcal{O}(t)}\sum_{k}c_k Z^{k}, \label{eq:graypullback}
    \end{equation}
    where $t\in\mathbb{Z}_4^n$ has $t_j=0,1,-1,2$ when the two bits of $a$ in block $j$ are $00,01,10,11$, respectively, and $\mathcal{O}(t)=\{j:t_j\text{ odd}\}$ is the odd support of $t$.  The partial inversion $R_{\mathcal{O}}=\bigotimes_{j\in\mathcal{O}}R_j$ negates the coordinates in $\mathcal{O}$: $R_{\mathcal{O}}|v\rangle=|v'\rangle$ with $v'_j=-v_j$ for $j\in\mathcal{O}$ and $v'_j=v_j$ otherwise.  The sum runs over the $k\in\mathbb{Z}_4^n$ with $k_j=0$, $k_j\in\{1,-1\}$, or $k_j=2$ when the two bits of $b$ in block $j$ are $00$, contain a single $1$, or are $11$, respectively, and all coefficients $c_k$ are nonzero.  Every term satisfies $|t|_{\mathrm L}=|a|_{\mathrm H}$ and $|k|_{\mathrm L}=|b|_{\mathrm H}$.
<!-- end lem-2 -->
<!-- proof-3: body_md -->
The bit flip $\sigma_x\otimes I$ exchanges the labels $00\leftrightarrow10$ and $01\leftrightarrow11$, i.e., it maps $|m\rangle$ to $|-m-1\rangle=X^{-1}R|m\rangle$, and $I\otimes\sigma_x$ maps $|m\rangle$ to $|1-m\rangle=XR|m\rangle$.  The phase flips $\sigma_z\otimes I$ and $I\otimes\sigma_z$ are diagonal with entries $(1,1,-1,-1)$ and $(1,-1,-1,1)$ for $m=0,1,2,3$, which are the displayed combinations of $Z=\mathrm{diag}(1,i,-1,-i)$ and $Z^{-1}$.  The remaining entries of \eqref{eq:graydictionary} are products of these.  Since $\Phi_Z$ is a tensor product, \eqref{eq:graypullback} follows by multiplying the single-block pullbacks, using that $R_j$ commutes with the Pauli operators of every other quartrit.  The weight statements hold because each block with one flipped bit contributes Lee weight one, and each block with two flipped bits contributes Lee weight two.
<!-- end proof-3 -->
<!-- thm-2: body_md -->
\label{thm:graylevels}
    Let $U$ be a $\mathbb{Z}_4$ Clifford gate on $n$ quartrits with symplectic matrix $M\in\mathrm{Sp}(2n,\mathbb{Z}_4)$, as in \eqref{eq:cliffordaction}, and let $\bar M$ be the reduction of $M$ modulo two.
    \begin{enumerate}
        \item $\Phi_ZU\Phi_Z^\dagger$ lies in the third level $\mathcal{C}_3$ of the Clifford hierarchy of $2n$ qubits.
        \item $\Phi_ZU\Phi_Z^\dagger$ is a qubit Clifford gate if and only if $\bar M$ is the symplectic matrix of a permutation of the quartrits.
        \item Every quartrit Pauli string maps to a qubit Clifford gate, and every even Pauli string $X^{2a}Z^{2b}$ maps to a qubit Pauli string.
    \end{enumerate}
    The same statements hold for every Pauli-basis Gray map $\Phi_{V,W}=\phi_{V,W}^{\otimes n}$.
<!-- end thm-2 -->
<!-- proof-4: body_md -->
Let $\mathcal{K}_n$ be the set of $\mathbb{Z}_4$ Clifford gates whose symplectic matrix is the identity modulo two.  Such a matrix has the form $M=I+2A$, and $(I+2A)(I+2B)=I+2(A+B)$ modulo four.  Thus, $\mathcal{K}_n$ is a group, and it is abelian modulo Pauli strings and phases.  It is normal in the $\mathbb{Z}_4$ Clifford group, because $M_U(I+2A)M_U^{-1}=I+2M_UAM_U^{-1}$.  For $M=I+2A$, the symplectic condition $M^{\mathsf T}JM=J$ modulo four says that $JA$ is symmetric modulo two.  The symmetric binary matrices are spanned by those with a single nonzero entry on the diagonal or a single symmetric pair of nonzero off-diagonal entries.  Each of these involves at most two of the $2n$ symplectic coordinates, hence at most two quartrits.  Modulo Pauli strings, $\mathcal{K}_n$ is therefore generated by gates on one or two quartrits, namely $R_j$, $S_j^2$, and $F_jS_j^2F_j^\dagger$ on one quartrit $j$, and $\mathrm{SUM}_{jk}^2$, $\mathrm{SUM}_{kj}^2$, $\mathrm{CZ}_{jk}^2$, and $(F_j\otimes F_k)\mathrm{CZ}_{jk}^2(F_j\otimes F_k)^\dagger$ on two quartrits $j$ and $k$.  Here, $\mathrm{CZ}=(I\otimes F)\,\mathrm{SUM}\,(I\otimes F^\dagger)$ is the quartrit controlled-$Z$ gate $|m,m'\rangle\mapsto i^{mm'}|m,m'\rangle$, distinct from the qubit $\mathrm{CZ}$ on the right-hand sides of \eqref{eq:kernelimages}.  Since $\Phi_Z$ is a tensor product, it suffices to compute the images of these generators on one or two blocks.  Label the two qubits of a block by $b_1b_2=\varphi(m)$, so that $m$ is even exactly when $b_1\oplus b_2=0$.  The inversion $R$ exchanges $m=1$ and $m=3$, i.e., the labels $01$ and $10$.  The gate $S^2=\mathrm{diag}(1,i,1,i)$ multiplies $|m\rangle$ by $i^{b_1\oplus b_2}$.  The gate $\mathrm{SUM}^2$ adds $2m$ to the target, which applies $X^2$ to the target when $m$ is odd, and $\mathrm{CZ}^2$ multiplies $|m,m'\rangle$ by $(-1)^{mm'}$, which depends only on the parities of $m$ and $m'$.  Finally, $FS^2F^\dagger=\tfrac{1+i}{2}I+\tfrac{1-i}{2}X^2$ and $(F\otimes F)\mathrm{CZ}^2(F\otimes F)^\dagger=\tfrac12(I+X^2\otimes I+I\otimes X^2-X^2\otimes X^2)$, since $FZ^2F^\dagger=X^2$.  Writing $j_p$ for qubit $p\in\{1,2\}$ of block $j$, we obtain
    \begin{equation}
    \begin{aligned}
        R&\mapsto\mathrm{SWAP}, &
        S^2&\mapsto(S_2\otimes S_2)\,\mathrm{CZ}, \\
        FS^2F^\dagger&\mapsto(H\otimes H)(S_2\otimes S_2)\,\mathrm{CZ}\,(H\otimes H), &
        \mathrm{SUM}_{jk}^2&\mapsto\prod_{p,p'=1}^{2}\mathrm{CNOT}_{j_p\to k_{p'}}, \\
        \mathrm{CZ}_{jk}^2&\mapsto\prod_{p,p'=1}^{2}\mathrm{CZ}_{j_pk_{p'}}, &
        (F_j\otimes F_k)\mathrm{CZ}_{jk}^2(F_j\otimes F_k)^\dagger&\mapsto H^{\otimes4}\prod_{p,p'=1}^{2}\mathrm{CZ}_{j_pk_{p'}}H^{\otimes4},
    \end{aligned} \label{eq:kernelimages}
    \end{equation}
    where $H^{\otimes4}$ acts on the four qubits of blocks $j$ and $k$.  These gates have the qubit Clifford images \eqref{eq:kernelimages}, and the quartrit Pauli strings have the qubit Clifford images listed in Table \ref{tab:grayimages}, which follow from \eqref{eq:graydictionary} (see below), so $\Phi_Z\mathcal{K}_n\Phi_Z^\dagger$ consists of qubit Clifford gates.

    By Lemma \ref{lem:graydictionary}, every qubit Pauli string pulls back to an element of $\mathcal{K}_n$ times a Pauli string: a bit flip on the first or second qubit of block $j$ pulls back to $X_j^{-1}R_j$ or $X_jR_j$, and a phase flip to $Z_j^{-1}S_j^2$ or $Z_jS_j^2$, respectively, since $Z^{-1}S^2=\mathrm{diag}(1,1,-1,-1)$ and $ZS^2=\mathrm{diag}(1,-1,-1,1)$.  For a $\mathbb{Z}_4$ Clifford gate $U$ and a qubit Pauli string $\sigma$, the operator $U(\Phi_Z^\dagger\sigma\Phi_Z)U^\dagger$ therefore lies in $\mathcal{K}_n$ times a Pauli string, by normality, and its image under $\Phi_Z$ is a qubit Clifford gate.  In other words, $(\Phi_ZU\Phi_Z^\dagger)\,\sigma\,(\Phi_ZU\Phi_Z^\dagger)^\dagger$ is a qubit Clifford gate for every qubit Pauli string $\sigma$, which is statement 1.  Statement 3 is the case $M=I$.  The images $X\mapsto(I\otimes\sigma_x)\,\mathrm{SWAP}$, $Z\mapsto(S_2^\dagger\otimes S_2)\,\mathrm{CZ}$, $X^2\mapsto\sigma_x\otimes\sigma_x$, and $Z^2\mapsto\sigma_z\otimes\sigma_z$ follow from \eqref{eq:graydictionary}.

    For statement 2, first suppose that $\bar M$ is the symplectic matrix of a permutation $\pi$ of the quartrits.  Then $U$ is the product of $\pi$, which $\Phi_Z$ maps to the same permutation of the blocks, and an element of $\mathcal{K}_n$, up to a Pauli string.  All three factors map to qubit Clifford gates.  Conversely, suppose that $\Phi_ZU\Phi_Z^\dagger$ is a qubit Clifford gate.  It then normalizes the qubit Pauli group, so $U$ normalizes the pullback of that group, which is generated, up to phases, by $X_j^{-1}R_j$, $X_jR_j$, $Z_j^{-1}S_j^2$, and $Z_jS_j^2$ for $j=1,\ldots,n$.  Their symplectic matrices are $I+2I_j$ for the bit flips and $I+2N_j$ for the phase flips.  Thus, the symplectic matrices of the pullback group are $I+2A$ with $A$ in the binary span $\mathcal{A}$ of the block identities $I_j$ and of the matrices $N_j$ that map $x_j$ to $z_j$.  Conjugation by $\bar M$ must preserve $\mathcal{A}$.  Now $\mathcal{A}$ is a commutative algebra isomorphic to the direct product of $n$ copies of $\mathbb{Z}_2[\varepsilon]/(\varepsilon^2)$, so its only primitive idempotents are the $I_j$.  An automorphism permutes them, so $\bar M$ maps the coordinate pair of quartrit $j$ onto that of quartrit $\pi(j)$ for some permutation $\pi$.  Moreover, $\bar M N_j\bar M^{-1}$ is a nonzero nilpotent element of $I_{\pi(j)}\mathcal{A}I_{\pi(j)}$, whose only such element is $N_{\pi(j)}$.  The resulting $2\times2$ block $B_j$ of $\bar M$ therefore commutes with $N=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\1&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)$.  The invertible binary matrices commuting with $N$ are $I$ and $I+N$, and $I+N$ is the symplectic matrix of the phase gate $S$ modulo two.  Thus, $U$ is the product of the permutation $\pi$, an element of $\mathcal{K}_n$, a Pauli string, and $S_j$ on some set of quartrits.  Since the first three factors have qubit Clifford images, $\Phi_ZU\Phi_Z^\dagger$ is a qubit Clifford gate only if this set is empty, because $\phi_ZS\phi_Z^\dagger=(T\otimes T)\,\mathrm{CS}=\mathrm{diag}(1,e^{i\pi/4},e^{i\pi/4},-1)$ is not a qubit Clifford gate: conjugating the bit flip $\sigma_x\otimes I$ on any one of these blocks already fails to give a Pauli string.  Finally, $\Phi_{V,W}U\Phi_{V,W}^\dagger=W^{\otimes n}\,\Phi_Z(V^{\dagger\otimes n}UV^{\otimes n})\Phi_Z^\dagger\,W^{\dagger\otimes n}$, where $V^{\dagger\otimes n}UV^{\otimes n}$ is again a $\mathbb{Z}_4$ Clifford gate, whose symplectic matrix modulo two is the symplectic matrix of a quartrit permutation exactly when $\bar M$ is, because the symplectic matrix of $V^{\otimes n}$ commutes with that of every quartrit permutation, and conjugation by the qubit Clifford gate $W^{\otimes n}$ preserves every level of the hierarchy.
<!-- end proof-4 -->
<!-- thm-3: body_md -->
\label{thm:graydistance}
    Let $Q$ be a stabilizer code on $n$ quartrits with $K\ge2$, and let $c_\diamond$ and $c_{\mathrm H}$ be the costs of Table \ref{tab:graycost}.  Then the joint Lee distance and the Hamming distance of $\Phi_Z(Q)$ satisfy
    \begin{equation}
        d\big(\Phi_Z(Q)\big)\ge\min_{L}\sum_{j=1}^n c_\diamond(L_j)
        \qquad\text{and}\qquad
        d_{\mathrm H}\big(\Phi_Z(Q)\big)\ge\min_{L}\sum_{j=1}^n c_{\mathrm H}(L_j), \label{eq:graydistance}
    \end{equation}
    where the minima run over the nontrivial logical Pauli strings $L=\bigotimes_jL_j$ of $Q$.  In other words, the joint Lee distance of the qubitized code is at least the joint Lee distance $d_\diamond$ of $Q$ recomputed with a discount of two at every site where the logical operator is $X^{\pm1}Z^2$.  For $\Phi_X$, the same holds with $X$ and $Z$ exchanged.
<!-- end thm-3 -->
<!-- proof-5: body_md -->
Let $E$ be a qubit Pauli string.  By Lemma \ref{lem:graydictionary}, its pullback $\Phi_Z^\dagger E\Phi_Z$ is a tensor product over blocks of single-quartrit operators, each of which is a linear combination of quartrit Pauli operators.  Expanding the product gives $\Phi_Z^\dagger E\Phi_Z=\sum_P\gamma_PP$, where every $P=\bigotimes_jP_j$ in the sum has $P_j$ in the expansion of the pullback of the part $E_j$ of $E$ on block $j$.  By the definition of the costs, $c(P_j)\le|E_j|$ in either metric, so $\sum_jc(P_j)\le|E|$.  For a quartrit Pauli string $P$, the operator $\Pi P\Pi$ vanishes if $P$ fails to commute with some stabilizer.  It is proportional to $\Pi$ if $P$ is a stabilizer up to a phase, and it is not proportional to $\Pi$ if $P$ is a nontrivial logical operator.  Hence, if $|E|$ is smaller than the minimum in \eqref{eq:graydistance}, then no nontrivial logical operator appears in the expansion, and $\Pi\Phi_Z^\dagger E\Phi_Z\Pi=\sum_P\gamma_P\Pi P\Pi$ is proportional to $\Pi$.  Every Pauli string of weight below the minimum is thus detectable, which proves \eqref{eq:graydistance}.  The costs in Table \ref{tab:graycost} are read off from the single-block pullbacks.  By \eqref{eq:graydictionary} and $R=\tfrac12(I+X^2+Z^2-X^2Z^2)$, a single bit flip contains $X^{\pm1}$ and $X^{\pm1}Z^2$, a single phase flip contains $Z^{\pm1}$, and a single $\sigma_y$ contains $X^{\pm1}Z^{\pm1}$.  The two-qubit operators $\sigma_x\otimes\sigma_x$, $\sigma_z\otimes\sigma_z$, $\sigma_y\otimes\sigma_y$, $\sigma_x\otimes\sigma_z$, and $\sigma_x\otimes\sigma_y$ contain $X^2$, $Z^2$, $X^2Z^2$, $X^{\pm1}Z^{\pm1}$, and $X^2Z^{\pm1}$, respectively.  Conversely, checking the sixteen two-qubit Pauli operators shows that none of smaller weight, in either metric, contains $P$, so the table entries are minima.  For $\Phi_X$, conjugation by $H$ exchanges $\sigma_x$ and $\sigma_z$ without changing weights, and conjugation by $F$ exchanges $X$ and $Z$ up to inversion.
<!-- end proof-5 -->
<!-- eg-12: body_md -->
\label{eg:grayyparity}
    The $[\![1,\log_42,2_\diamond]\!]_4$ code with the single stabilizer $X^2Z^2$ detects every error of joint Lee weight one.  Its logical operators are the Pauli operators $X^aZ^b$ with $a\equiv b\pmod 2$, and the lightest nontrivial ones, $X^{\pm1}Z^{\pm1}$, $X^2$, and $Z^2$, have joint Lee weight two.  Among them, $X^{\pm1}Z^{\pm1}$ has separate Lee weight one, so $d_\diamond=2$ and $d_\square=1$.  By Table \ref{tab:grayimages}, the stabilizer maps to $(\sigma_x\otimes\sigma_x)(\sigma_z\otimes\sigma_z)=-\sigma_y\otimes\sigma_y$.  The qubitized code is thus the two-qubit $Y$-parity code $[\![2,1,2]\!]$ of Section \ref{subsec:qubits}, up to the sign of its check, whose joint Lee distance is two and whose Hamming distance is one.  Both bounds of Theorem \ref{thm:graydistance} are attained, since $X^{\pm1}Z^{\pm1}$ costs two in the joint Lee metric and one in the Hamming metric.  The joint Lee metric, which counts a $\sigma_y$ error twice, thus reproduces the distance of the quartrit code, whereas the Hamming metric counts it once and loses a factor of two.
<!-- end eg-12 -->
<!-- thm-4: body_md -->
\label{thm:graycss}
    Let $Q$ be a CSS code on $\mathbb{Z}_4$ with $K\ge2$, distances $d_X$ and $d_Z$, codespace projector $\Pi$, and $X$-check module $\mathcal{D}=\mathrm{im}(H_X^{\mathsf T})$.  Define
    \begin{subequations} \label{eq:munu}
    \begin{align}
        \mu_X &= \min\big\{|s|_{\mathrm L} : s\in\mathcal{D},\ \Pi R_{\mathcal{O}(s)}\Pi\not\propto\Pi\big\}, \label{eq:muX}\\
        \nu_X &= \min\big\{|s|_{\mathrm L}+|z_{\operatorname{supp}(s)^{\mathrm c}}|_{\mathrm L} : s\in\mathcal{D}\setminus0,\ z\in\ker(H_X)\setminus\mathrm{im}(H_Z^{\mathsf T})\big\}, \label{eq:nuX}
    \end{align}
    \end{subequations}
    with the minimum of an empty set taken to be $\infty$.  Thus, $\mu_X=\infty$ if no $s$ qualifies, and $\nu_X=\infty$ if $\mathcal{D}=\{0\}$.  Then, in the joint Lee and Hamming metrics,
    \begin{equation}
        \min(d_X,d_Z,\nu_X)\le d_{\mathrm H}\big(\Phi_Z(Q)\big)\le d\big(\Phi_Z(Q)\big)\le\min(d_X,d_Z,\mu_X). \label{eq:graycss}
    \end{equation}
    Moreover, $\nu_X\ge\lceil d_Z/2\rceil$, and $\nu_X$ is at least the minimum Lee weight of a nonzero vector in $\mathcal{D}$.
<!-- end thm-4 -->
<!-- proof-6: body_md -->
The lower bound follows from Theorem \ref{thm:graydistance}.  A nontrivial logical operator of a CSS code is $X^tZ^z$ with $t\in\ker(H_Z)$ and $z\in\ker(H_X)$, where $t\notin\mathcal{D}$ or $z\notin\mathrm{im}(H_Z^{\mathsf T})$.  By Table \ref{tab:graycost}, $c_{\mathrm H}(X^{t_j}Z^{z_j})$ equals $|t_j|_{\mathrm L}$ whenever $t_j\neq0$, and equals $|z_j|_{\mathrm L}$ otherwise.  If $t\notin\mathcal{D}$, the Hamming cost of $X^tZ^z$ is at least $|t|_{\mathrm L}\ge d_X$.  If $t=0$, it is $|z|_{\mathrm L}\ge d_Z$.  If $t\in\mathcal{D}\setminus0$ and $z\notin\mathrm{im}(H_Z^{\mathsf T})$, it is $|t|_{\mathrm L}+|z_{\operatorname{supp}(t)^{\mathrm c}}|_{\mathrm L}\ge\nu_X$.  This proves the first inequality in \eqref{eq:graycss}, and the second is \eqref{eq:leevshammingdiamond}.

    For the upper bound, we use pure bit-flip and phase-flip strings, whose Hamming and joint Lee weights coincide.  First, let $Z^z$ be a nontrivial $Z$-type logical operator with $|z|_{\mathrm L}=d_Z$.  By \eqref{eq:graydictionaryz} read in reverse, $Z\mapsto\tfrac{1+\mathrm{i}}{2}\,\sigma_z\otimes I+\tfrac{1-\mathrm{i}}{2}\,I\otimes\sigma_z$ and $Z^2\mapsto\sigma_z\otimes\sigma_z$, so $\Phi_ZZ^z\Phi_Z^\dagger$ is a combination of phase-flip strings of Hamming weight $d_Z$.  Since $\Pi Z^z\Pi$ is not proportional to $\Pi$, at least one of these strings is undetectable.  Second, let $X^t$ be a nontrivial $X$-type logical operator with $|t|_{\mathrm L}=d_X$, and let $\sigma_x^{a}$ be the bit-flip string of Hamming weight $d_X$ whose pullback is $X^tR_{\mathcal{O}(t)}$.  Applied to the codeword $|\bar0\rangle$, the pullback yields $|\mathcal{D}|^{-1/2}\sum_{c\in\mathcal{D}}|t+c'\rangle$, where $c'$ is $c$ with the coordinates in $\mathcal{O}(t)$ negated.  The term with $c=0$ shows that this vector has a nonzero overlap with the codeword $|\bar t\rangle$, which is orthogonal to $|\bar0\rangle$ because $t\notin\mathcal{D}$.  Hence $\sigma_x^a$ is undetectable.  Third, if $\mu_X<\infty$, let $s\in\mathcal{D}$ attain $\mu_X$, and let $\sigma_x^{a}$ be the bit-flip string whose pullback is $X^sR_{\mathcal{O}(s)}$.  Since $X^{-s}$ is a stabilizer, $\Pi X^s=(X^{-s}\Pi)^\dagger=\Pi$, and $\Pi X^sR_{\mathcal{O}(s)}\Pi=\Pi R_{\mathcal{O}(s)}\Pi$ is not proportional to $\Pi$.

    For the last statement, the case $\mathcal{D}=\{0\}$ follows from the convention on empty minima.  Otherwise, let $s$ and $z$ attain $\nu_X$, and let $\mathcal{T}=\operatorname{supp}(s)$.  Then $|z_{\mathcal{T}^{\mathrm c}}|_{\mathrm L}\ge d_Z-|z_{\mathcal{T}}|_{\mathrm L}\ge d_Z-2|\mathcal{T}|$ and $|s|_{\mathrm L}\ge|\mathcal{T}|$, so $\nu_X\ge\max(|\mathcal{T}|,d_Z-|\mathcal{T}|)\ge d_Z/2$.  The bound $\nu_X\ge\min_{s\in\mathcal{D}\setminus0}|s|_{\mathrm L}$ is immediate from \eqref{eq:nuX}.
<!-- end proof-6 -->
<!-- cor-1: body_md -->
\label{cor:semiselfdual}
    If $\mathrm{im}(H_X^{\mathsf T})\subseteq\mathrm{im}(H_Z^{\mathsf T})$, then $\nu_X\ge d_Z$, and the qubitized code $\Phi_Z(Q)$ has joint Lee distance and Hamming distance both equal to $\min(d_X,d_Z)$.  If instead $\mathrm{im}(H_Z^{\mathsf T})\subseteq\mathrm{im}(H_X^{\mathsf T})$, the same holds for $\Phi_X(Q)$.
<!-- end cor-1 -->
<!-- proof-7: body_md -->
Let $s\in\mathcal{D}\setminus0$ and $z\in\ker(H_X)\setminus\mathrm{im}(H_Z^{\mathsf T})$, and let $\mathcal{T}=\operatorname{supp}(s)$.  By assumption, $Z^s$ is a stabilizer, so $Z^{z+as}$ is a nontrivial $Z$-type logical operator for every $a\in\mathbb{Z}_4$, and $|z+as|_{\mathrm L}\ge d_Z$.  On each coordinate $j\in\mathcal{T}$, the four values $z_j+as_j$ have Lee weights summing to four: they exhaust $\mathbb{Z}_4$ if $s_j$ is odd, and they are $z_j$ and $z_j+2$ twice each if $s_j=2$.  Summing over $\mathcal{T}$ and averaging over $a$, we find some $a$ with $|(z+as)_{\mathcal{T}}|_{\mathrm L}\le|\mathcal{T}|\le|s|_{\mathrm L}$.  Since $z+as$ agrees with $z$ outside $\mathcal{T}$,
    \begin{equation}
        d_Z\le|z+as|_{\mathrm L}\le|z_{\mathcal{T}^{\mathrm c}}|_{\mathrm L}+|s|_{\mathrm L},
    \end{equation}
    which gives $\nu_X\ge d_Z$.  Theorem \ref{thm:graycss} then yields $\min(d_X,d_Z)\le d_{\mathrm H}\le d\le\min(d_X,d_Z)$.  The mirror statement follows by applying $F^{\otimes n}$, which exchanges the two check matrices up to inversion.
<!-- end proof-7 -->
<!-- prop-4: body_md -->
\label{prop:grayimage}
    Let $Q$ be a CSS code on $\mathbb{Z}_4$ with $\mathcal{D}=\mathrm{im}(H_X^{\mathsf T})$ and $\mathcal{C}=\ker(H_Z)$.  Then $\Phi_Z(Q)$ is a qubit stabilizer code if and only if
    \begin{enumerate}
        \item $2\,u\ast v\in\mathcal{C}$ for all $u,v\in\mathcal{C}$, i.e., $\varphi(\mathcal{C})$ is linear, and
        \item $2\,u\ast c\in\mathcal{D}$ for all $u\in\mathcal{C}$ and $c\in\mathcal{D}$.
    \end{enumerate}
    In that case, $\Phi_Z(Q)$ is the qubit CSS code with $X$-type checks generating $\varphi(\mathcal{D})$ and $Z$-type checks generating $\varphi(\mathcal{C})^\perp$, and, if $K\ge2$, its distance is $\min(d_X,d_Z)$ in both metrics.  For $\Phi_X$, exchange the roles of $H_X$ and $H_Z$.
<!-- end prop-4 -->
<!-- proof-8: body_md -->
The codewords of $Q$ map to $|\mathcal{D}|^{-1/2}\sum_{c\in\mathcal{D}}|\varphi(\mu+c)\rangle$, uniform superpositions over the classes $\varphi(\mu+\mathcal{D})$, which partition $\varphi(\mathcal{C})$.  Applying the identity $\varphi(u+v)=\varphi(u)\oplus\varphi(v)\oplus\varphi(2\,u\ast v)$ twice gives $\varphi(\mu+c)=\varphi(\mu)\oplus\varphi(c+2\,\mu\ast c)$.  If condition 2 holds, then $c\mapsto c+2\,\mu\ast c$ maps $\mathcal{D}$ into itself and is an involution, since $(1+2\mu_j)^2=1$ modulo four on each coordinate.  It is therefore a bijection of $\mathcal{D}$, so $\varphi(\mu+\mathcal{D})=\varphi(\mu)\oplus\varphi(\mathcal{D})$, and condition 2 with $u\in\mathcal{D}$ makes $\varphi(\mathcal{D})$ linear.  With condition 1, $\varphi(\mathcal{C})$ is a linear code containing $\varphi(\mathcal{D})$, the classes are the cosets of $\varphi(\mathcal{D})$ in $\varphi(\mathcal{C})$, and the codewords are exactly those of the qubit CSS code built from $\varphi(\mathcal{D})\subseteq\varphi(\mathcal{C})$.

    Conversely, suppose that $\Phi_Z(Q)$ is a stabilizer code.  The set of computational basis states on which its projector has a nonzero diagonal entry is the set of strings on which all $Z$-type stabilizers have eigenvalue $+1$, which is an affine subspace.  Here this set is $\varphi(\mathcal{C})$, which contains $0$, so $\varphi(\mathcal{C})$ is linear, which is condition 1.  Moreover, for two strings $x$ and $y$ in this set, the matrix element $\langle x|\Pi|y\rangle$ of a stabilizer code is nonzero if and only if $x\oplus y$ is the bit-flip part of a stabilizer.  Here it is nonzero if and only if $x$ and $y$ lie in the same class.  Every class $\varphi(\mu+\mathcal{D})$ is therefore a coset of one fixed subspace, namely $\varphi(\mathcal{D})$, so $\varphi(\mu+c)\oplus\varphi(\mu)=\varphi(c)\oplus\varphi(2\,\mu\ast c)$ lies in $\varphi(\mathcal{D})$ for all $c\in\mathcal{D}$.  Hence $\varphi(2\,\mu\ast c)\in\varphi(\mathcal{D})$, which is condition 2.

    For the distance, assume $K\ge2$.  The $X$-type logical operators of the qubit CSS code are the strings in $\varphi(\mathcal{C})\setminus\varphi(\mathcal{D})$, whose minimal Hamming weight is $d_X$ because $\varphi$ is an isometry.  Its $Z$-type distance is at most $d_Z$ by the phase-flip construction in the proof of Theorem \ref{thm:graycss}, and at least $d_Z$ because a phase-flip string of Hamming weight below $d_Z$ pulls back to a combination of $Z^k$ with $|k|_{\mathrm L}<d_Z$, each of which is detectable or a stabilizer.
<!-- end proof-8 -->
<!-- defn-6: body_md -->
A quantum CSS code specified by
$H_X:\mathbb Z_q^n\rightarrow\mathbb Z_q^{m_X}$ and
$H_Z:\mathbb Z_q^n\rightarrow\mathbb Z_q^{m_Z}$ is a
$(w_{\mathrm B},w_{\mathrm C})$-Lee-LDPC code if both $H_X$ and $H_Z$
satisfy Eq.~\eqref{eq:classicalLee}.
<!-- end defn-6 -->
<!-- prop-5: body_md -->
\label{prop:commteoverZ}
    For any $(w_{\mathrm{B}},w_{\mathrm{C}})$-Lee-LDPC CSS code defined over $\mathbb{Z}_q$, if $q > w_{\mathrm{C}}^2$ and we lift each entry of $H_X$ and $H_Z$ to $\mathbb{Z}$, then $H_XH_Z^{\mathsf{T}}=0$ holds over $\mathbb{Z}$, not only $\mathbb{Z}_q$.
<!-- end prop-5 -->
<!-- proof-9: body_md -->
Since both $X$-checks and $Z$-checks have Lee weight at most $w_{\mathrm{C}}$, over $\mathbb{Z}$ we know that the matrix $H_XH_Z^{\mathsf{T}}$ is entrywise bounded:  $|(H_XH_Z^{\mathsf{T}})_{a,b}|\le w_{\mathrm{C}}^2$.  
On the other hand, the CSS commutation condition says that each entry $(H_XH_Z^{\mathsf T})_{a,b}$, i.e., the inner product of an $X$-check with a $Z$-check,
vanishes modulo $q$. Hence, when $q>w_{\mathrm C}^2$, it must vanish exactly, even over $\mathbb{Z}$.
<!-- end proof-9 -->
<!-- prop-6: body_md -->
\label{prop:decom}
For any quantum CSS code defined by parity-check matrices $H_X$ and $H_Z$, if $H_X H_Z^\mathsf{T}=0$ over $\mathbb{Z}$, we can directly obtain the decomposition of the codespace $\mathcal{C}$ from the SNFs of $H_X$ and $H_Z$,
\begin{align}\label{eq:SNF-H}
  U_X H_X V_X &= D_X
  = \operatorname{diag}\bigl(d_1^X,\ldots,d_{r_X}^X,0,\ldots,0\bigr), \nonumber\\
  U_Z H_Z V_Z &= D_Z
  = \operatorname{diag}\bigl(d_1^Z,\ldots,d_{r_Z}^Z,0,\ldots,0\bigr),
\end{align}
where $r_{X/Z}$ is the rank of $H_{X/Z}$. The codespace is isomorphic to   \begin{align}\label{eq:C-decomp_integer}
    \mathcal{C}
    \cong
    \mathbb{Z}_q^{\,n - r_X - r_Z}
    \;\oplus\;
    \bigoplus_{j=1}^{r_Z}
      \mathbb{Z}_{\gcd(d_{j}^Z,\,q)}
    \;\oplus\;
    \bigoplus_{i=1}^{r_X}
      \mathbb{Z}_{\gcd(d_{i}^X,\,q)}.
  \end{align}
We note that any factor $\mathbb{Z}_{\gcd(d_i^{X/Z},q)}$ with $\gcd(d_i^{X/Z},q)=1$ is trivial and can be omitted. 

There exist logical dual bases $x_L$ and $z_L$ such that
their pairing is diagonal, the entry for a cyclic summand $\mathbb Z_g$ being $q/g$ (so $1$ for each free summand), as in \eqref{eq:logicalConjugates}. Thus each cyclic summand in the
decomposition is associated with a unique logical pair. The dual bases also satisfy the following
properties. For each $i$ with $\gcd(d_i^X,q)>1$, the corresponding logical $X$ operator, denoted $x_{L,a}$,
lies in the real linear span of $X$ stabilizers, while its dual logical
$z_{L,a}$, viewed as an integer vector, satisfies
$H_X z_{L,a} = q c_a$ for some nonzero integer vector $c_a$. Similarly,
for each $j$ with $\gcd(d_j^Z,q)>1$, the corresponding logical $Z$ operator, denoted $z_{L,b}$,
lies in the real linear span of $Z$ stabilizers, while its dual
logical $x_{L,b}$ satisfies
$H_Z x_{L,b} = q c_b$ for some nonzero integer vector $c_b$.
<!-- end prop-6 -->
<!-- proof-10: body_md -->
The proof is provided in Appendix \ref{app:codespace_integer}. The same argument in fact applies more generally: it is enough that there exist
integer matrices congruent modulo $q$ to the present $H_X$ and $H_Z$ and
satisfying exact commutation over $\mathbb Z$. Replacing $H_X$ and $H_Z$
throughout the proof by those integer matrices gives exactly the same
decomposition result. To avoid introducing additional notation, however, we
state and use only the simpler version here.
<!-- end proof-10 -->
<!-- cor-2: body_md -->
\label{cor:reducedweightbound}


For any quantum $(w_{\mathrm{B}},w_{\mathrm{C}})$-Lee-LDPC code with $H_X H_Z^\mathsf{T}=0$ over $\mathbb{Z}$, let $\alpha_{X/Z}\le w_{\mathrm C}$ denote the largest Lee weight of a single entry of $H_{X/Z}$. Then the logical dual bases $x_L$ and $z_L$ described in Proposition \ref{prop:decom} have the following properties. For each $i$ with $\gcd(d_i^X,q)>1$, the reduced Lee weight (the smallest Lee weight up to stabilizers) of the corresponding logical $X$ operator $x_{L,a}$ is at most $w_{\mathrm C} n /2$, while the reduced Lee weight of its dual logical $z_{L,a}$ is at least $q/\alpha_X$. For each $j$ with $\gcd(d_j^Z,q)>1$, the reduced Lee weight of the corresponding logical $Z$ operator $z_{L,b}$ is at most $w_{\mathrm C} n /2$, while the reduced Lee weight of the dual logical $x_{L,b}$ is at least $q/\alpha_Z$.
<!-- end cor-2 -->
<!-- proof-11: body_md -->
For every \(i\) with \(\gcd(d_i^X,q)>1\), the corresponding basis element \(x_{L,a}\) lies in \(\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb{R}}(H_X^{\mathsf T})\). Choose \(r_X\leq n\) linearly independent original \(X\)-check rows \(h_1,\ldots,h_{r_X}\) spanning this real row space, and write
\begin{equation}
x_{L,a}=\sum_{k=1}^{r_X}\lambda_k h_k.
\end{equation}
For each \(k\), choose an integer \(m_k\) such that \(|\lambda_k-m_k|\le 1/2\). Subtracting the stabilizer combination \(\sum_k m_k h_k\) gives a representative of the same logical class with Lee weight at most
\begin{equation}
\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{r_X}|h_k|_{\mathrm{L}}
\leq
\frac{w_{\mathrm{C}}r_X}{2}
\leq
\frac{w_{\mathrm{C}}n}{2}.
\end{equation}

Now consider its dual logical $z_{L,a}$ that satisfies $H_X z_{L,a} = q c_a$. Here $c_a$ is a nonzero integer vector for any representative of $z_{L,a}$. If the reduced Lee weight of $z_{L,a}$ were strictly smaller than $q/\alpha_X$, we would get
\begin{equation}
\left|(H_X z_{L,a})_i\right|
\le
\sum_j |(H_X)_{ij}|_L\, |(z_{L,a})_j|_L
\le
\alpha_X \sum_j |(z_{L,a})_j|_L
=
\alpha_X |z_{L,a}|_L <q,
\end{equation}
which is impossible because $c_a \neq 0$, so the reduced Lee weight of $z_{L,a}$ is at least $q/\alpha_X$.

The same argument applies to every $j$ with $\gcd(d_j^Z,q)>1$. This completes the proof.
<!-- end proof-11 -->
<!-- prop-7: body_md -->
\label{prop:trivialcodespace}
For any quantum CSS code over $\mathbb Z_q$, suppose that $H_XH_Z^{\mathsf T}=0$
over $\mathbb Z$. Let the nonzero Smith factors of $H_X$ and $H_Z$ be
$d_1^X,\ldots,d_{r_X}^X$ and $d_1^Z,\ldots,d_{r_Z}^Z$. Then the codespace over
$\mathbb Z_q$ is trivial if and only if
\begin{equation}
    r_X+r_Z=n,
    \qquad
    \gcd(d_i^X,q)=1 \text{ for all } i,
    \qquad
    \gcd(d_j^Z,q)=1 \text{ for all } j.
\end{equation}
Moreover, if the $\mathbb Z_q$ codespace is nontrivial, then either there
exists a nontrivial logical $Z$ operator $z_L$ and an integer vector
$\widetilde z_L\in\ker_{\mathbb Z}H_X$ such that
\(
    \widetilde z_L \bmod q = z_L,
\)
or there exists a nontrivial logical $X$ operator $x_L$ and an integer vector
$\widetilde x_L\in\ker_{\mathbb Z}H_Z$ such that
\(
    \widetilde x_L \bmod q = x_L.
\)
<!-- end prop-7 -->
<!-- proof-12: body_md -->
By \eqref{eq:C-decomp_integer} in Proposition~\ref{prop:decom}, the codespace is trivial exactly when $r_X+r_Z=n$ and all cyclic factors
in the two torsion blocks are trivial.

If the codespace is nontrivial, at least one of the three summands above is
nontrivial. If the free summand $\mathbb Z_q^{\,n-r_X-r_Z}$ is nontrivial, then
$n-r_X-r_Z>0$. By the decomposition \eqref{eq:decom_kerHX} established in the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:decom} (Appendix~\ref{app:codespace_integer}), the
integer kernel of $H_X$ splits as
\begin{equation}
    \ker_{\mathbb Z} H_X
    =
    \operatorname{Sat}(\operatorname{im}_{\mathbb Z} H_Z^{\mathsf T})
    \oplus F_Z,
\end{equation}
where $F_Z$ is a free abelian group of rank $n-r_X-r_Z$. The image
$\operatorname{im}_{\mathbb Z}(H_Z^{\mathsf T})$ is contained in the first
summand, so any $\widetilde z_L\in F_Z$ whose reduction $z_L:=\widetilde z_L \bmod q$ is nonzero represents a nontrivial logical $Z$ operator in
\(
    \ker_{\mathbb Z_q} H_X / \operatorname{im}_{\mathbb Z_q} H_Z^{\mathsf T}.
\)
Alternatively, one could use the dual free summand in $\ker_{\mathbb Z}H_Z$ to obtain a nontrivial logical $X$ operator with an exact integer-kernel representative.

On the other hand, if $\gcd(d_j^Z,q)>1$ in the Smith decomposition \eqref{eq:SNF-H}, the vector
\(
    \widetilde z_L=(V_Z^{-1})^{\mathsf T}e_j
\)
satisfies $d_j^Z\widetilde z_L\in\operatorname{im}_{\mathbb Z}(H_Z^{\mathsf T})$ and
\(
    H_X\widetilde z_L=0.
\)
Thus $z_L :=\widetilde z_L\bmod q\in\ker_{\mathbb Z_q}H_X$.

It remains to check that $z_L$ is not a $Z$ stabilizer.
Suppose, for contradiction, that
\(
    z_L\in\operatorname{im}_{\mathbb Z_q}(H_Z^{\mathsf T}).
\)
Then there exist integer vectors $a$ and $b$ such that
\(
    \widetilde z_L=H_Z^{\mathsf T}a+qb.
\)
Multiplying by $V_Z^{\mathsf T}$, and looking at the $j$-th coordinate, gives
\(
    1=d_j^Z c+qb'
\)
for some integers $c,b'$, which forces $\gcd(d_j^Z,q)=1$, contradicting $\gcd(d_j^Z,q)>1$.
Therefore
$z_L \in \ker_{\mathbb Z_q} H_X / \operatorname{im}_{\mathbb Z_q} H_Z^{\mathsf T}$ is a nontrivial $Z$-logical class, and it has an
integer lift $\widetilde z_L\in\ker_{\mathbb Z}H_X$.

The $X$-side torsion case is identical. If $\gcd(d_i^X,q)>1$, there exists a nontrivial logical $X$ operator $x_L$ with an
integer lift $\widetilde x_L\in\ker_{\mathbb Z}H_Z$.

This proves the claim.
<!-- end proof-12 -->
<!-- lem-3: body_md -->
\label{lem:smallbarrier}
Let $H\in\mathbb Z^{m\times n}$ be an integer matrix satisfying
\begin{equation}
    \sum_{i=1}^m |H_{ij}| \le w_{\mathrm B}
    \qquad
    \text{for each } j=1,\ldots,n.
\end{equation}
For $x\in\mathbb Z^n$, define the energy
\begin{equation}
    E(x)=|Hx|_1.
\end{equation}
Then, for every $v\in\ker_{\mathbb Z}H$, there exists a path
\begin{equation}
    0=x^{(0)},x^{(1)},\ldots,x^{(T)}=v
\end{equation}
in $\mathbb Z^n$, where each step changes a single coordinate by $+1$ or $-1$,
such that
\begin{equation}
    E(x^{(t)})<2w_{\mathrm B} n
\end{equation}
for every $t$. Moreover, reducing the entire path modulo any $q\ge 2$ gives a
path in $\mathbb Z_q^n$ from $0$ to $v\bmod q$, and the Lee weight of the
syndrome along this reduced path is also bounded by $2w_{\mathrm B} n$.
<!-- end lem-3 -->
<!-- proof-13: body_md -->
Fix $v\in\ker_{\mathbb Z}H$. Then $H\alpha v=0$ over $\mathbb{R}$ for any $\alpha\in\mathbb{R}$. For each $\alpha\in[0,1]$, let
\begin{equation}
    x(\alpha)=\lfloor \alpha v\rfloor,
\end{equation}
where the floor is taken componentwise. The point of this construction is that each coordinate of $x(\alpha)-\alpha v$ has absolute value less than one.
Therefore,
\begin{equation}
    |Hx(\alpha)|_1
    \le
    \sum_{a,j}|H_{aj}|\,|x_j(\alpha)-\alpha v_j|
    <
    \sum_j\sum_a |H_{aj}|
    \le
    w_{\mathrm B} n.
\end{equation}
Thus every rounded point $x(\alpha)$ has energy strictly smaller than $w_{\mathrm B} n$.

It remains to turn these rounded points into a single-site path. As $\alpha$
increases from $0$ to $1$, the vector $x(\alpha)$ changes only when one of the
numbers $\alpha v_j$ crosses an integer. Listing these changes in increasing
order gives a finite sequence of rounded configurations
\begin{equation}
    0=y^{(0)},y^{(1)},\ldots,y^{(M)}=v,
\end{equation}
such that each coordinate of $y^{(r+1)}-y^{(r)}$ lies in $\{-1,0,1\}$. We now
connect consecutive rounded configurations by an explicit single-site path. For
a fixed $r$, let $j_1,\ldots,j_s$ be the coordinates on which
$y^{(r+1)}-y^{(r)}$ is nonzero, and write
\begin{equation}
    \sigma_\ell
    =
    \bigl(y^{(r+1)}-y^{(r)}\bigr)_{j_\ell}
    \in \{-1,1\}.
\end{equation}
Define
\begin{equation}
    y^{(r,\ell)}
    =
    y^{(r)}
    +
    \sum_{\mu=1}^{\ell}\sigma_\mu e_{j_\mu},
    \qquad
    \ell=0,\ldots,s.
\end{equation}
Then $y^{(r,0)}=y^{(r)}$, $y^{(r,s)}=y^{(r+1)}$, and each step from
$y^{(r,\ell)}$ to $y^{(r,\ell+1)}$ changes exactly one coordinate by $+1$ or
$-1$. Concatenating these paths over all $r$ gives a single-site path from $0$
to $v$.

A single-site update at coordinate $j$ changes the syndrome by one column of
$H$, with a sign. Hence the energy can change by at most
\begin{equation}
    \sum_a |H_{aj}|\le w_{\mathrm B}.
\end{equation}

Between $y^{(r)}$ and $y^{(r+1)}$, at most $n$ coordinates are updated. Since
$E(y^{(r)})<w_{\mathrm B}n$, every intermediate configuration on this short segment has
energy less than
\begin{equation}
    w_{\mathrm B}n+w_{\mathrm B}n=2w_{\mathrm B}n.
\end{equation}

Concatenating these short single-site paths gives a path from $0$ to $v$ along
which every configuration has energy less than $2w_{\mathrm B}n$. Relabeling the concatenated path as $x^{(0)},\ldots,x^{(T)}$ gives the claimed path.

Finally, reducing this integer path modulo any $q\ge 2$ gives a single-site path
in $\mathbb Z_q^n$ from $0$ to $v\bmod q$. At each step, the Lee weight of the
reduced syndrome is no larger than the ordinary $\ell_1$ weight of the
corresponding integer syndrome, so the same $2w_{\mathrm B}n$ energy bound holds after
reduction modulo $q$.
<!-- end proof-13 -->
<!-- thm-5: body_md -->
\label{thm:energybarrier}
Consider a quantum $(w_{\mathrm B},w_{\mathrm C})$-Lee-LDPC CSS code over $\mathbb Z_q$ on $n$
physical qudits, defined by parity-check matrices $H_X$ and $H_Z$. Suppose that
the code has nontrivial logical operators. Then either the $Z$-error sector or
the $X$-error sector contains a nontrivial logical operator whose energy barrier is
bounded by
\begin{equation}
    \Delta E
    \le
    \begin{cases}
        w_{\mathrm B}w_{\mathrm C}^2 n/2, & q\le w_{\mathrm C}^2, \\
        2w_{\mathrm B}n, & q>w_{\mathrm C}^2.
    \end{cases}
\end{equation}
In particular, the energy barrier is at most
linear in $n$, with a constant not
growing with $q$.
<!-- end thm-5 -->
<!-- proof-14: body_md -->
The proof is trivial when $q\le w_{\mathrm C}^2$ because every configuration has energy at most $w_{\mathrm B}n\lfloor q/2\rfloor\le w_{\mathrm B}w_{\mathrm C}^2n/2$: $H_X$ and $H_Z$ each have at most $w_{\mathrm B}n$ nonzero rows (their total Lee weight is at most $w_{\mathrm B}n$), and each syndrome entry has Lee weight at most $\lfloor q/2\rfloor$. When $q>w_{\mathrm C}^2$, from Proposition \ref{prop:commteoverZ}, the code must have $H_X H_Z^\mathsf{T}=0$ over $\mathbb{Z}$. In this case, by Proposition \ref{prop:trivialcodespace}, nontriviality of the codespace implies that either there is an integer vector $\widetilde z_L\in\ker_{\mathbb Z}H_X$
whose reduction modulo $q$ is a nontrivial logical $Z$ operator $z_L$, or there
is an integer vector $\widetilde x_L\in\ker_{\mathbb Z}H_Z$ whose reduction
modulo $q$ is a nontrivial logical $X$ operator $x_L$.

In the first case, apply Lemma~\ref{lem:smallbarrier} to $H_X$ and
$\widetilde z_L$. It gives a path from $0$ to
$\widetilde z_L$ over $\mathbb Z$, where each step changes a single coordinate by $+1$ or $-1$. The energy is always less than
$2w_{\mathrm B}n$ in the path. Reducing the entire path modulo $q$ gives a path from the zero error configuration to the nontrivial logical $Z$ operator $z_L$, and the energy on $\mathbb{Z}_q$ is still less than $2w_{\mathrm B} n$.
The second case is identical, which gives the claimed bound on the energy barrier.
<!-- end proof-14 -->
<!-- thm-6: body_md -->
\label{thm:baddistance}
 For any quantum $(w_{\mathrm{B}},w_{\mathrm{C}})$-Lee-LDPC code on $\mathbb{Z}_q$, let $L_X \in \ker(H_Z)$ and $L_Z \in \ker(H_X)$ be nontrivial noncommuting logical operators, namely $L_X\cdot L_Z\ne 0 \pmod{q}$. Then there exist either $\widetilde{L}_X\in \ker(H_Z)$ or $\widetilde{L}_Z\in \ker(H_X)$ such that  $\widetilde{L}_X\cdot L_Z\ne 0 \pmod{q}$ [or $L_X\cdot \widetilde{L}_Z\ne 0 \pmod{q}$] and 
\begin{equation} \label{eq:baddistancebound}
    \min\left(|\widetilde{L}_X|_{\mathrm{L}}, |\widetilde{L}_Z|_{\mathrm{L}}\right)\le
    \begin{cases}
        \tfrac12 w_{\mathrm C}^2 n, & q\le w_{\mathrm C}^2,\\[2mm]
        \left(2\sqrt{2}+\frac{w_{\mathrm C}}{2}\right)n,
        & w_{\mathrm C}^2<q.
    \end{cases}
\end{equation}
<!-- end thm-6 -->
<!-- proof-15: body_md -->
When $q\le w_{\mathrm C}^2$, the proof is trivial. For $q>w_{\mathrm C}^2$, from Proposition \ref{prop:commteoverZ}, the code must have $H_X H_Z^\mathsf{T}=0$ over $\mathbb{Z}$. The proof of this part is nontrivial and is given in Appendix~\ref{app:baddistance}; here we provide an executive summary.  The essential strategy is to start from the reduced weight of $L_X$,\begin{equation}
        \min_{k_X\in\mathbb Z_q^{m_X}}|L_X+H_X^{\mathsf{T}}k_X|_{\mathrm{L}},
    \end{equation} and relax the minimization by allowing $k_X$ to range over $\mathbb{R}^{m_X}$, which is easier to analyze mathematically.  This relaxation, along with relaxing norms from $\ell_1$ to $\ell_2$, incurs at most $O(n)$ loss in the final Lee-weight bound.  We then generalize mathematical results known as transference inequalities for dual lattices~\cite{transference, 2017thesis} to prove the bound \eqref{eq:baddistancebound} for every noncommuting pair of logical operators.
<!-- end proof-15 -->
<!-- defn-7: body_md -->
\label{def:hgp}
Let $H_1\in\mathbb Z_q^{m_1\times n_1}$ and
$H_2\in\mathbb Z_q^{m_2\times n_2}$ be the parity-check matrices of two codes. The hypergraph-product
code associated with $(H_1,H_2)$ is the CSS code with check matrices
\begin{equation}
    H_X=
    \left(
        H_1\otimes I_{n_2}
        \,\middle|\,
        I_{m_1}\otimes H_2^{\mathsf T}
    \right),
    \qquad
    H_Z=
    \left(
        -I_{n_1}\otimes H_2
        \,\middle|\,
        H_1^{\mathsf T}\otimes I_{m_2}
    \right). \label{eq:hgp}
\end{equation}
These matrices naturally satisfy
\(
    H_XH_Z^{\mathsf T}=0,
\)
so they define a CSS code over $\mathbb Z_q$ on
\(
    n_1 n_2+m_1 m_2
\)
physical qudits.
<!-- end defn-7 -->
<!-- eg-13: body_md -->
\label{eg:leetoric}
    Let $q=2^r$, let $\lambda\in\mathbb{Z}_q$ satisfy $\lambda^2-\lambda+2=0$ as in Example~\ref{eg:liftedhamming}, and let $V=\mathrm{circ}_L(v)$ be the $L\times L$ circulant matrix whose rows are the cyclic shifts of
    \begin{equation}
        v=(1,\ \lambda,\ \lambda-1,\ -1,\ 0,\ \dots,\ 0), \label{eq:leetoricpattern}
    \end{equation}
    the coefficients of the reciprocal $x^3g_q(x^{-1})$ of the lifted-Hamming generator \eqref{eq:liftedhamming}.  The hypergraph product of $V$ with itself places one qudit on each horizontal and each vertical edge of an $L\times L$ torus.  Each $X$ check is a vertex term with two arms of four edges, and each $Z$ check is a plaquette term with two such arms, with the exponents shown in Fig.~\ref{fig:leetoric}.  Every check acts on eight edges (six for $q=2$, where $\lambda=0$) inside a $4\times4$ window, for every $q$, and for $q=4$, where $\lambda=2$, the arms read $X,X^2,X,X^{-1}$.

\definecolor{toricx}{RGB}{23,110,94}
\definecolor{toricz}{RGB}{86,70,168}
\definecolor{toricgrid}{RGB}{200,205,200}
    \begin{figure}[t]
        \centering
        \begin{tikzpicture}[x=0.8cm, y=0.8cm,
          op/.style={midway, fill=white, inner sep=1.1pt, font=\scriptsize}]
        \begin{scope}
        \foreach \yy in {-4,...,1}{\draw[toricgrid] (-1,\yy) -- (5,\yy);}
        \foreach \xx in {-1,...,5}{\draw[toricgrid] (\xx,-4) -- (\xx,1);}
        \draw[gray!70, dashed] (-0.16,-4.16) rectangle (4.16,0.16);
        \draw[toricx, very thick] (0,0) -- (1,0) node[op]{$1$};
        \draw[toricx, very thick] (1,0) -- (2,0) node[op]{$\lambda$};
        \draw[toricx, very thick] (2,0) -- (3,0) node[op]{$\lambda-1$};
        \draw[toricx, very thick] (3,0) -- (4,0) node[op]{$-1$};
        \draw[toricx, very thick] (0,0) -- (0,-1) node[op]{$1$};
        \draw[toricx, very thick] (0,-1) -- (0,-2) node[op]{$\lambda$};
        \draw[toricx, very thick] (0,-2) -- (0,-3) node[op]{$\lambda-1$};
        \draw[toricx, very thick] (0,-3) -- (0,-4) node[op]{$-1$};
        \fill (0,0) circle (2.6pt);
        \node[font=\small] at (2,-5.1) {(a) vertex term, $X$ type};
        \end{scope}
        \begin{scope}[xshift=8.6cm]
        \foreach \yy in {-4,...,1}{\draw[toricgrid] (-4,\yy) -- (2,\yy);}
        \foreach \xx in {-4,...,2}{\draw[toricgrid] (\xx,-4) -- (\xx,1);}
        \draw[gray!70, dashed] (-3.16,-4.16) rectangle (1.16,0.16);
        \draw[toricz, very thick] (0,-3) -- (1,-3) node[op]{$1$};
        \draw[toricz, very thick] (0,-2) -- (1,-2) node[op]{$\lambda$};
        \draw[toricz, very thick] (0,-1) -- (1,-1) node[op]{$\lambda-1$};
        \draw[toricz, very thick] (0,0) -- (1,0) node[op]{$-1$};
        \draw[toricz, very thick] (0,-4) -- (0,-3) node[op]{$-1$};
        \draw[toricz, very thick] (-1,-4) -- (-1,-3) node[op]{$-\lambda$};
        \draw[toricz, very thick] (-2,-4) -- (-2,-3) node[op]{$1-\lambda$};
        \draw[toricz, very thick] (-3,-4) -- (-3,-3) node[op]{$1$};
        \fill (0,-3) circle (2.6pt);
        \node[font=\small] at (-1,-5.1) {(b) plaquette term, $Z$ type};
        \end{scope}
        \end{tikzpicture}
        \caption{\textbf{Checks of the lifted-Hamming toric code.}  One $\mathbb{Z}_q$ qudit sits on each edge, and the number on an edge is the exponent of the Pauli operator applied to it, with $\lambda$ a root of $\lambda^2-\lambda+2$ in $\mathbb{Z}_q$ as in Example~\ref{eg:liftedhamming}.  (a) The $X$ check at a vertex (dot) applies $X^{1},X^{\lambda},X^{\lambda-1},X^{-1}$ along four edges to the east and four edges to the south.  (b) The $Z$ check at a plaquette corner (dot) applies the same exponents to four horizontal edges to the north and the negated exponents to four vertical edges to the west, in the row just below the dot.  Every check occupies the dashed $4\times4$ window, for every $q$.}
        \label{fig:leetoric}
    \end{figure}

    The code is nontrivial exactly when $7$ divides $L$.  Its string operators carry the elements of the annihilator of $v$ in $\mathbb{Z}_q[x]/(x^L-1)$, and $v$ is a unit of this ring unless $7\mid L$, because $v$ reduces to an irreducible cubic modulo two.  For every $L=7m$, the annihilator is a free module of rank three (for odd $m$, it is the Galois-ring factor of $\mathbb{Z}_q[x]/(x^L-1)$ that belongs to $v$, i.e., $\mathbb{Z}_q[x]/(v)\cong\mathrm{GR}(q,3)$), and the code encodes $k=2\cdot3^2=18$ qudits of dimension $q$ (verified numerically for $L=7,14$ and $q\le64$).  The annihilator consists of the codewords of the dual $\mathcal{H}_q^\perp$ of the lifted Hamming code, repeated with period seven, so a row or column carrying a minimum-weight codeword of $\mathcal{H}_q^\perp$ is a logical operator.  Hence
    \begin{equation}
        d\le\frac{d_qL}{7},\qquad d_q=4,\,6,\,8,\,12,\,14,\,24\quad\text{for}\quad q=2,\,4,\,8,\,16,\,32,\,64, \label{eq:leetoricd}
    \end{equation}
    where $d_q$ is the minimum Lee weight of $\mathcal{H}_q^\perp$.  For $q=4$ and $L=7$, exhaustive enumeration of all errors of Lee weight at most five shows that \eqref{eq:leetoricd} is attained, so the code is a $[\![98,18,6]\!]_4$ code.  Its Hamming distance is four, because the code reduced modulo two is the hypergraph product of the binary $[7,3,4]$ simplex code with itself.  Thus, the octacode's ratio $3/2$ of Lee to Hamming distance survives in two dimensions, against $[\![98,2,7]\!]_4$ for the $\mathbb{Z}_4$ toric code on the same torus.  Whether the distance equals $d_qL/7$ for all $L$ and $q$ is open.

    Increasing $r$ in $q=2^r$ keeps the check support at eight edges, while both the check Lee weight $2(2+|\lambda|_{\mathrm L}+|\lambda-1|_{\mathrm L})$ and the string weight $d_q$ per period grow: $|\lambda|_{\mathrm L}\gtrsim\sqrt q$ because, for the centered representative of $\lambda$, the integer $\lambda^2-\lambda+2$ is positive and divisible by $q$, hence at least $q$, and $d_q\ge\sqrt q$ because an annihilator word $c$ evaluated at a primitive seventh root of unity has algebraic norm at least $q^3$ and at most $|c|_{\mathrm L}^6$ in absolute value.  The strings thus grow heavier without bound in $q$ at fixed geometry, at the price of check Lee weights that grow at least as $\sqrt q$.  By the definition of Section~\ref{sec:LDPC}, which requires $w_{\mathrm C}$ to be independent of $q$, this family is therefore Lee-LDPC only at fixed $q$.

    For $q=4$, the topological order of the code is that of nine $\mathbb{Z}_4$ toric codes, but its lattice translations permute the anyons.  It is thus a symmetry-enriched topological order \cite{Barkeshli2019SET} whose enriching symmetry is translation~\cite{Watanabe2023toric,Chen2025anyon}.  To describe the permutation, we use the Galois ring $\mathrm{GR}(4,3)=\mathbb{Z}_4[x]/(h(x))$, where $h(x)=-v(x)=x^3-(\lambda-1)x^2-\lambda x-1$ (for $q=4$, $h=x^3+3x^2+2x+3$) is the monic cubic factor of $x^7-1$ over $\mathbb{Z}_4$ that reduces to the irreducible $x^3+x^2+1$ modulo two \cite[Sec.~II]{Hammons1994}, \cite[Ch.~6]{Wan1997quaternary}.
